# Proto-Elamite: strategies log

One row per strategy: method and control, result, verdict. Earlier work is summarised in FINDINGS.md.

| ID | Method and control | Result | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| pe2:PE-1.1 | Assignment score (mean cosine of 60 pairs), all features; C1 = each feature column shuffled within each script (200x) | real 0.702 vs C1 mean 0.657, max 0.682 (p < 0.005) | beats C1, but C1 only shows both scripts share a feature covariance structure (e.g. sole-sign <-> header); it does not show correct pairings |
| pe2:PE-2.1 | Same score; C2 = PE numeral groups shuffled between entries of the same tablet (30x), profiles rebuilt | real 0.702 vs C2 mean 0.704, max 0.712 (p = 0.70) | NULL: destroying the PE sign-system link does not lower the score. The pairing is not driven by commodity/number-system behaviour |
| pe2:PE-3.1 | Same without position features (PC transliteration order is editorial) | real 0.736; C1 mean 0.723 (p = 0.055); C2 mean 0.739 (p = 0.63) | NULL on both controls |
| pe2:PE-4.1 | Planted, within proto-cuneiform: PC tablets split at random, one half relabelled and sub-sampled to 1,585 tablets (PE size); top-60 relabelled signs assigned into top-200 of the other half (5 seeds) | 14, 9, 16, 19, 18 of 60 recovered (mean 15.2 = 25%; chance ~0.3) | method CAN recover identity when both halves come from one script and one archive mix |
| pe2:PE-5.1 | Planted, cross-site: Uruk tablets as reference, non-Uruk PC tablets (Jemdet Nasr, Umma, uncertain) relabelled | 4 of 60 recovered (7%) | recovery collapses when archives differ even inside one script; a cross-script bridge between Uruk and Susa is expected to recover far less |
| pe2:PE-6.1 | Per-pair p (assigned similarity vs PE sign's best similarity against column-shuffled PC, 200x) | 6 of 60 pairs p < 0.01 (M388-NAM2, M297-SZIM, M066-PIRIG, M206-ZATU753, M048-AN, M002-TAR); ~0.6 expected by chance under this lenient null | lenient null only; tested against held-out data and stability in cycle 2 |
| pe2:PE-1.2 | Stability: pair from half A and from half B separately (all features); share of PE signs with same PC partner. Reference: two halves of PC paired into PC | PE 12.7% of signs keep their partner; PC self-halves 72.5% | PE partners are mostly unstable; only M388 (10/10), M376 (9/10), M297 (8/10), M124 (7/10) keep a partner |
| pe2:PE-2.2 | Held-out commodity test: pair on A WITHOUT own-system features (position, tablet context, header, reverse, magnitude); rank of partner among 200 PC signs by similarity of PC system profile to the PE sign's system profile on held-out B (0 = best, random = 0.5). Control: same pairing after PE-A numeral groups shuffled within tablet | real mean rank 0.423, top-10% 19.3%; control 0.404, top-10% 21.7% | NULL: the pairing predicts held-out number-system behaviour no better than the control. The prediction that exists (rank < 0.5) comes from tablet context, which the shuffle keeps |
| pe2:PE-3.2 | Known-function partners (PC sign names: grain, beer, small/large cattle, person, fish, textile...): is the PE sign's held-out capacity share above the PE baseline exactly when the partner's is above the PC baseline? | 53/88 sign-split cases (60%; binomial p = 0.04, but cases repeat across splits and the null is not uniform) | weak, not independent; proper null in cycle 3 |
| pe2:PE-4.2 | Per-sign leaders: no-system partner mode and held-out top-10% count | M036 -> TAR 8/10, top-10% 10/10; M002 -> GUG2 6/10, 8/10; M243 -> NIN 5/10, 8/10; M010 -> |ZATU714xHI@g| 4/10, 8/10; M376 -> AL 9/10 but held-out 2/10; M388 -> NAM2 9/10 but held-out 2/10 | the signs that predict well are the PE capacity class (M036, M002, M243, M010, M297) -- class-level role, partners vary; needs a per-sign null (cycle 3) |
| pe2:PE-1.3 | Per-sign held-out test, all PC, K_PC = 200 (60 PE signs) | 0 signs p < 0.01; 1 p < 0.05 (M003-GIR3, partner mode only 2/10); ~3 expected by chance. Mean rank real 0.423 vs null 0.406 | NULL |
| pe2:PE-2.3 | Cycle-2 leaders against their own null | M036-TAR rank 0.040 vs null 0.052 (p = 0.13); M002-GUG2 0.057 vs 0.051 (p = 0.75); M243-NIN 0.116 vs 0.112; M010 0.100 vs 0.085; M297 0.114 vs 0.057 (p = 1.0); M376-AL 0.127 vs 0.128; M388-NAM2 0.304 vs 0.314 | their good held-out ranks are reproduced by shuffled-system pairings: the information is the tablet context (capacity tablets), not the partner identity. Cycle-2 B candidates demoted |

The method works inside one archive but cannot carry identities across sites, let alone across scripts |
| pe2:PE-2.F | Held-out commodity prediction (pair without system features, predict number system on unseen PE tablets), per-sign null | 0/60 signs p < 0.01 in all three settings (all PC K=200, Uruk III only, K=100); 0-2 p < 0.05 (chance ~3) | NULL |

| pe4:PE-2.4 | PE multi-sign middles, all classes pooled (836 entries, 142 tablets, 62 tokens), 1,000 permutations | S/null 1.06 (p 0.35), 1/62 tokens p < 0.01 (0.6 expected; M346 +1.03, a final-class sign inside a middle) | NULL at a size where real attribute codes score 2.8-3.6 |
| pe4:PE-3.4 | By system: counted (SDB, any class, 719 entries, 49 tokens); capacity (C, 73 entries, 1 usable token) | SDB S/null 0.94 (p 0.60), 0/49; C untestable (only M387 qualifies, p 0.99) | counted middles: no scale information; capacity middles: too few multi-sign entries to say |
| pe6:PE-1.1 | Prior predictive coverage, first prior (500 runs) | real outside the 1-99% prior range on H_excl (opener signs also occur in entries: 0.52 vs sim 1.00), H_div, D_uniq, D_types, D_multi | first economy too rigid: roles were exclusive and every name came from a roster. Added polyvalence (p_poly), one-off names (p_oneoff), name-only headers (p_hdr_open), n_syl up to 1,500; bank restarted |
| pe6:PE-2.1 | Prior predictive coverage, final prior (20,000 runs) | all 43 real statistics inside the 1-99% prior band (closest: E_inicon q 0.03, D_multi q 0.05, H_len q 0.06) | the prior can make Proto-Elamite-like corpora; fit is meaningful |
| pe6:PE-3.1 | ABC fit, rejection (best 1% = 200) + local-linear regression; distance in MAD units | min distance 4.75, median accepted 6.5 | fitted (see economy below) |

NOT identifiable (r < 0.45): n_off 0.40, w_mean 0.22, a_off 0.03, p_poly 0.04, n_open 0.37, n_pre 0.41, n_cc 0.42, n_co 0.42, a_goods 0.23, p_open_off 0.11. 90% interval coverage 0.76-0.89 (slightly overconfident) | the corpus pins the WRITING practices and quantity scales; it does NOT pin the size of the institution (offices, workers per office) |
| pe6:PE-5.1 | Fitted writing practices (posterior median [5-95%]) | headers on 69% of tablets [60-77]; only 32% [25-40] of headers start with an opener; ONE opener sign [1-2]; entries per tablet 6.6 [5.4-8.1]; 83% [72-91] of tablets list named units; a named entry carries a class sign only 27% [12-44] of the time; prefix on 5% [1-11]; bare-numeral sub-lines 12% [5-29]; names 1.7 signs [1.4-2.5] from a large name-sign stock (~1,140 [906-1,316]); 72% [28-95] of names one-off; totals on 53% [37-74] of eligible tablets, 59% [42-80] signed with the class sign, 63% [41-72] of totals not equal to the sum | the fake Susa writes like this: one general opener, mostly named-unit lists, class sign usually omitted after a name, totals often off |
| pe6:PE-6.1 | Fitted economy | offices 12.5 [2-49]; named units per office 7.6 [2-104]; goods per office 5 [2-10]; counted goods 29 [7-54], capacity goods 3 [1-17]; counted quantity median 2.9 (log-sd 1.95); capacity quantity median 40 [18-106] N39C units (~1.7 N39B; ~1/3 of the N01 capacity unit in the a-priori set), log-sd 2.3; 53% [41-59] of entries switch good within a tablet | office count and roster size are prior-dominated (PE-4.1): no claim. Quantities are pinned but are per-ENTRY, not per-head rations: the model cannot separate rations from deliveries |
| pe6:PE-7.1 | Posterior predictive check (200 new runs at posterior draws), all 43 statistics | outside 95% band: E_len2 (real 0.17 vs sim 0.25), N_Ssd (0.70 vs 0.64), D_types (549 vs ~1,060 sign types); borderline H_len (q 0.035), X_frag (q 0.025) | MISFIT on sign inventory: the fake Susa needs ~2x more sign types than PE has to reach PE's string uniqueness. PE makes its one-off strings out of fewer signs, i.e. more combination per sign than a 'name = random draw from a syllable stock' model |
| pe6:PE-8.1 | CONTROL shuffled corpus: all sign tokens permuted over the corpus, same ABC | distance 5.50 (real 4.75; median accepted 7.7 vs 6.5); posterior moves: p_cls 0.27 -> 0.05, p_pre 0.05 -> 0.01, n_pre 2 -> 22, p_totsign 0.59 -> 0.36, k_goods 5 -> 2, p_poly 0.24 -> 0.54 | the shuffle is fitted only somewhat worse (whole-corpus statistics carry little sign-identity information); the parameters that move are exactly the sign-slot practices (class sign, prefix, signed totals). The economy-level numbers barely move |

| pe7:PE-1.2 | Does family structure add tree signal? Father+son samples vs unrelated seal owners of the same period | UR3_PAT RI excess 0.097 vs UR3_SEAL 0.103; OB_PAT 0.119 vs OB_SEAL 0.110; clade support likewise equal | tree-likeness in Mesopotamian names comes from the shared name-element lexicon (compounding), NOT from kinship: test (1) alone cannot detect lineage; tests (2)-(3) are needed |
| pe7:PE-1.3 | Cluster sample (whole tablets) vs random sample of PE names | RI excess 0.041 vs 0.024; delta drop 0.034 vs 0.024; support 0.022 vs 0.014 | names written on the same tablet raise tree signal: co-tablet similarity exists (tested directly in cycle 2) |
| pe7:PE-1.4 | Fitted mutation spectrum (hard-EM stochastic edit model, nearest-neighbour alignments, match/sub/indel) | PE 0.39/0.31/0.30 (indel total); Ur III 0.46-0.50/0.30-0.32/0.20-0.22; OB 0.46-0.48/0.34-0.36/0.16-0.20; LINB 0.47/0.26/0.26; RAND 0.38/0.29/0.34; CN 0.35/0.65/0 | PE nearest neighbours are as far apart as random strings (match share 0.39 vs 0.38 random, 0.46+ in real name lists): PE middles are not built from a small stock of recurring name elements |
| pe6:PE-1.2 | REAL corpus, naive grades (A >= 0.9, B >= 0.7) | 164 signs: 98 SYL (A/B), 19 CLS_S (A/B: M072 0.92, M418 0.91, M346, M206, M264, M261, M374, M354, M348, M367, M317, M029, M372, M376, M223, |M370+M072|, M302, M320, M318); CLS_C top role but p < 0.7: M002 0.69, M050, M297 0.48, M379, M243 0.44, M036 0.39, M044; NO sign assigned OPEN or PRE (M157 -> SYL 0.88; M387 -> SYL 0.60; M370 -> SYL 0.95) | only the class layer comes out; no opener, no prefix |

Grades must be calibrated to the shuffle null (cycle 4). Real signs above the shuffle max (0.744): 10 CLS_S (M072, M418, M346, M206, M264, M261, M374, M354, M348, M367) |
| pe6:PE-3.2 | CONTROL planted: tablets written by known economies (posterior median; a far posterior draw; a prior draw near the data), whole pipeline rerun | median economy: 165 signs, 151 A + 11 B + 3 C, all 100% correct (CLS_S 15/15, PRE 2/2, CLS_O 2/2, CLS_C 1/2, OPEN 0/1 confident); far draw: 219/219 correct; near-data prior draw: A 27/27, B 35/36, C 56/58 correct (PRE 19/20, OPEN 2/2) | the method recovers roles when the tablets really come from this kind of economy. | pe6:PE-6.2 | Why no opener? Posterior economy has 1 opener sign and 68% of headers written as a bare NAME | a header-dominant real sign (M157: 85% of tokens in headers, 84% header-first) is classed as a much-used office NAME, not an opener | MODEL ARTEFACT, not a finding about M157: the fitted header practice lets busy office names mimic an opener. Earlier direct tests (FINDINGS d) still stand |
| pe6:PE-1.3 | Leave-one-statistic-out, 43 statistics | 40/43 predicted (q 0.05-0.95). Fail: E_inicon q 0.035 (only 31% of entry-initial tokens go to the top 10 signs; predicted 61%), N_MIfin q 0.04 (final sign carries 0.31 bits about the number system; predicted 0.95), H_len q 0.015 (headers 1.26 signs; predicted 2.2) | the economy, fitted to everything else, predicts most of PE's shape (tablet size, damage, totals, header rate, string uniqueness, Zipf slope) |
| pe6:PE-2.3 | The three failures read as model statements | (a) PE entry openings are much more varied than 'prefix or name' allows; (b) the commodity-to-number-system link is about 3x WEAKER than in a world where each good has its own number system: PE class signs mix systems (M288 34% capacity, M297 54%); (c) headers are short: an opener mostly alone, not opener + office name | (b) is the strongest: PE number systems follow the TABLET (FINDINGS attack 2) more than the good. The fake Susa's 'one good = one system' rule is wrong for Susa |
| pe6:PE-3.3 | Leave-one-GROUP-out (60 new runs per group) | L, T, D, X, H groups predicted (all q 0.05-0.95 except H_len 0.05); E group: all predicted (E_len2 q 0.07); N group: N_MIfin q 0.017 again, others predicted | the number-system group is the one part that the rest of the corpus cannot reproduce; everything else is mutually consistent |
| pe6:PE-4.3 | Role assignment with the N, E or H group left out of the fit (60 runs, 5 forests) | the same class signs come back each time: without N: M264 0.92, M418 0.90, M261 0.88, M072 0.84, M050 CLS_C 0.75, M002 CLS_C 0.67, M354, M346, M367; without H: M418, M072, M261, M264, M002, M050, M346, M206, M354, M367; without E: weaker (max 0.74) but same set; still no OPEN, no PRE | class assignments do not depend on any single statistic group being in the fit; they rest on the per-sign profile (standing alone before a numeral, numeral system) |
| pe6:PE-1.4 | Null thresholds (3 shuffles x 164 signs) | CLS_S 99% 0.77 / max 0.82; CLS_C 0.22 / 0.32; SYL 0.997 / 0.998; OPEN 0.003 / 0.006; PRE 0.02 / 0.03; CLS_O 0.13 / 0.14; CLS_F 0.07 / 0.08 | a shuffled corpus routinely gives 'class sign' probabilities up to 0.8: naive cut-offs are meaningless for CLS_S |
| pe6:PE-2.4 | CONTROL: the grading itself applied to shuffle 1 against shuffles 2+3 | 0 non-name signs above the 99% or max threshold | the calibrated grade has no false positives on shuffled data |
| pe6:PE-3.4 | Real grades, class layer | A: M072 CLS_S 0.90, M346 CLS_S 0.83, M002 CLS_C 0.67, M297 CLS_C 0.53 (both halves, and within-line shuffle keeps them). B: M418 CLS_S 0.91 (too rare in one half), M206 CLS_S 0.77, M050 CLS_C 0.60, M379 CLS_C 0.51, M243 CLS_C 0.47, M036 CLS_C 0.43. C (indistinguishable from shuffled signs): 37 more CLS_S incl. M264, M354, M367, M376, M288, M388 | the fake Susa recovers the counted/capacity class split already found directly (FINDINGS test b: M002, M297, M243, M036 capacity-tied; M346 never capacity). Restatement, not a new gloss |
| pe6:PE-4.4 | Real grades, name layer | A (more name-like than any shuffled sign, both halves): M099, M246, M136, M251, M254, M304, M005, M262, M340, M390, |M106+M288|; B: M319, M328, M352, M384 | B: these signs behave like the fake Susa's name-spelling stock (spread over many tablets, inside multi-sign strings, mixed systems). M099 and M384 are herd-name components in the herd office (pe5); M136 is a header sign (FINDINGS d), here read as an office/unit NAME |
| pe6:PE-5.4 | Roles never assigned to any real sign | OPEN, PRE, CLS_F, CLS_O: 0 signs at any calibrated grade. Diagnostic: in posterior simulations, signs that dominate headers are names 182 times and openers 66 times | the fitted header practice (1 opener, 68% name-only headers) lets office names imitate openers, so M157 is read as a name (SYL 0.88). Prefix (M387, M370, M124) and fraction (M376) roles are not recovered: the posterior has prefixes on 5% of entries and 0 fraction goods. Absence of these roles is a model failure, not evidence against them |
| pe6:PE-1.F | Agent-based economy (offices, named units, goods with number systems, rations/deliveries) + scribal practices; 38 parameters; ABC (rejection 1% + local-linear regression) on 43 statistics; prior-predictive coverage; parameter-recovery control on 200 held-out runs | all 43 statistics inside the prior band; recovery r >= 0.75 for 18 writing/quantity parameters, r < 0.45 for office count, workers per office, opener/prefix/capacity-good counts | FITTED. The corpus pins HOW the scribes wrote, not HOW BIG the institution was |
| pe6:PE-2.F | Fitted economy (posterior median [5-95%]) | offices 12.5 [2-49] and named units per office 7.6 [2-104] (both prior-dominated); 5 goods per office; ~29 counted vs ~3 capacity goods; 6.6 entries per tablet; 83% of tablets list named units; class sign written after a name only 27% of the time; names ~1.7 signs, 72% one-off; headers on 69% of tablets but an opener on only a third of them; totals on 53%, 63% of them not equal to the sum; capacity entries median 40 N39C units [18-106] (~1/3 of the N01 capacity unit, a-priori set), counted entries median 2.9 | 'ration level' is a per-entry capacity amount; the model cannot separate rations from deliveries. Staff size: no claim |
| pe6:PE-3.F | Posterior predictive + leave-one-statistic-out | 40/43 statistics predicted from the rest. Fail: final-sign -> number-system information (0.31 bits observed vs 0.95 predicted), entry-initial diversity, header length; sign inventory (PE reaches its string uniqueness with half the sign types the model needs) | the economy's 'one good = one number system' rule is wrong for Susa: the system follows the tablet more than the commodity sign (agrees with attack 2) |
| pe6:PE-4.F | Role assignment, with controls: global shuffle x4 (calibration + false-positive check: 0), within-line shuffle, planted economies x3 (100% / 100% / 97% of confident roles correct), random halves | calibrated A: class signs M072, M346 (counted), M002, M297 (capacity); name-spelling signs M099, M246, M136, M251, M254, M304, M005, M262, M340, M390, |M106+M288|. B: M418, M206 (counted), M050, M379, M243, M036 (capacity); M319, M328, M352, M384 (names). 37 other 'class' signs are C (no better than shuffled signs) | the class layer is a RESTATEMENT of FINDINGS test b (no new gloss). The name layer is new as a grade but rests on one model. No opener, prefix, fraction or other-system role is recovered for any sign (model artefact: M157 is read as an office name) |
| pe7:PE-2.2 | Recovery: 15 planted families of 4 (founder = a real PE middle, 1/2/3 mutations each under the fitted PE edit rates) replace 60 of 200 PE names | m=1: 8/8, ratio 1.34, dist z 9.2, co z 30.8; m=2: 8/8, 1.21, 5.9, 16.3; m=3: 8/8, 1.16, 4.7, 11.3 | a lineage of PE-like names 3 edits deep is recovered every time |
| pe7:PE-3.2 | PE tablet label (whole-tablet cluster samples) vs negatives: Linear B personnel tablets, random strings carrying the PE tablet labels | PE: PS 8/8, ratio 1.125, dist z 2.6 (6/8), co z 7.8; LINB: 6/8, ratio 1.043, dist z 1.9, co z 3.1; RAND: 0/8, 0.994, 0.6, 0.1 | REAL co-tablet clustering in PE: names on one tablet resemble each other, ~3x the Linear B effect, about the size of a planted 3-mutation lineage covering 30% of names; edit-distance gap only 2.0 bits (planted: 3.6-6.2) |
| pe7:PE-4.2 | Header, volume (tablet-level perm, same tablet excluded), class sign, numeral system (name-level) | header dist z -0.7, co 1.1; volume -0.2, -0.1; class -0.2, 2.5; system -1.0, 1.7 (PS of header/volume 6/8 only via the tablet effect) | no clustering by header, by excavation volume or by commodity office beyond the tablet itself; class sign weak (co only) |
| pe7:PE-5.2 | Herd office (pe5 ledger). First run flagged names containing M362 (circular: shared sign); rerun with flag = pe5 herd-office tablets only, see cycle 3 rows | M362-flag run: PS 8/8 ratio 1.93, pair dist z 3.5 | M362-flag result is DISCARDED as circular (the flag is itself a shared element) |
| pe7:PE-2.3 | Whole-corpus kin transmission, positive: Ur III seal father->son, 2,585 pairs; OB 909 pairs; share of pairs sharing >= 1 element vs fathers permuted | Ur III 0.231 vs 0.208 (lift 1.11, z 3.2); OB 0.388 vs 0.309 (lift 1.25, z 6.2); carried by theophoric / title elements (Ur III en x7, Nanna x10, Nanshe x6; OB Utu x3.5, Ishkur x7, Enlil x7) | real naming systems pass on elements, weakly: 10-25% excess sharing |
| pe7:PE-3.3 | Co-tablet sharing, whole corpus, names permuted among tablet slots (2,000x). PE (258 tablets, 1,055 name slots); negatives: Ur III Drehem admin names on one tablet (pe4 'ki PN-ta' set, not kin), Linear B personnel tablets | Ur III admin 0.172 vs 0.178 (lift 0.97, p 0.68); Linear B 0.290 vs 0.258 (lift 1.13, z 5.1; carried by -ja, -wo, -wa endings = name-class morphology); PE 0.301 vs 0.125 (lift 2.42, z 19); same first sign x4.6, same last sign x4.8; unchanged without the herd office (lift 2.37) | PE co-tablet resemblance is 10x the father-son excess of Ur III and 6x OB in relative terms. Kinship of Mesopotamian strength cannot produce it; it is a tablet-level shared-element effect |
| pe7:PE-4.3 | Which signs carry it (per-sign co-tablet lift, p <= 0.001) | M388 (264 vs 109), M124 (217 vs 47), M370 (76 vs 6), M054 (77 vs 21), M001 (28 vs 6), |M217+M388| (36 vs 0.3), |M288+1(N01)| (28 vs 0.2), |M136+M365| (21 vs 0.3), M376, M075, M149, M103, M145 | carriers include a numeral compound (|M288+1(N01)|) and frequent framing signs: a tablet's middles share a local qualifier more than a family name |
| pe7:PE-5.3 | Grading by tree homoplasy (per-character RI on best trees, PE real vs shuffled) and its validation on Ur III pairs (does per-char RI predict which elements fathers pass to sons?) | PE per-char RI 0.248 vs 0.194 shuffled (top excess M346, M059, M010, M367, M054); validation FAILS: Ur III per-char RI vs father-son excess Spearman 0.06 (p 0.51, 120 elements) | tree-based element grading does not detect inheritance even where inheritance is known; it measures compounding coherence. No PE element can be graded 'inherited' from trees |
| pe7:PE-2.4 | Name variants? Share of co-tablet sharing pairs where one middle is a subsequence of the other (same name +/- a sign) | PE 36 of 1,252 (2.9%; null 3.4 of 516); Linear B 1.2% | not one person written twice; the sharers are different strings |
| pe7:PE-3.4 | Coverage: per tablet (>= 4 names) elements shared by >= 2 names, share of the tablet's names carrying each. Reference: same tablets after global shuffle with planted kin groups of 2-3 sharing one element; Linear B | PE 3.6 shared elements per tablet, median coverage 0.33, top 0.54; planted kin 3.8, 0.33, 0.54; Linear B 7.5, 0.19, 0.40 | PE coverage looks exactly like planted small kin groups (several partial clusters, no single tablet-wide element): CONSISTENT with households, not decisive |
| pe7:PE-4.4 | Line-distance decay of sharing within tablets vs within-tablet order shuffle (500x). Positive: planted kin blocks written on adjacent lines | planted blocks: adjacent 0.76 vs 0.53 (p 0.002), distant pairs below null; PE: adjacent 0.387 vs 0.377 (p 0.18), 2 lines 0.377 vs 0.365, 5+ 0.256 vs 0.259; Linear B flat | PE sharing groups are NOT written as adjacent blocks. If they are kin groups, the scribes did not list kin together; more likely a tablet-local stock of name signs |
| pe7:PE-5.4 | Herd office, flag = pe5 herd-office tablets only (12 multi-sign names, 8 reps of trees); cross-tablet pairs only, edit distance vs 2,000 random 12-name sets | tree PS p < 0.05 in 8/8 (nested in the tablet effect); cross-tablet mean 19.2 bits vs 24.6 random (p 0.035); 7 of 12 names carry M362 (|M362+X| compounds), M207/M059/M005/M260 recur | herd names resemble each other across herd tablets through the M362 compound and a few recurring signs: a herd-naming pattern (herd = M362-compound + unit sign), not a demonstrated lineage. C-grade only |
| pe7:PE-1.F | (1) Tree-likeness vs shuffled-element null (delta, CI/RI of best Fitch tree, MCMC clade support; 6 reps x 200 names) | RI excess: PE 0.024 (z 3.6), Linear B 0.021, Ur III/OB names 0.10-0.12, Chinese 0.17, random 0.005; family samples (UR3/OB father+son) no more tree-like than unrelated names; PE nearest-neighbour match share 0.39 = random strings (0.38) vs 0.46+ real name lists | PE names are weakly tree-like (Linear B level). Tree-likeness in names reflects a shared element lexicon, not kinship, so (1) cannot by itself show lineage |
| pe7:PE-2.F | (2) Do clades map onto tablets, headers, offices, the herd office? Fitch label score + pair tests (8 reps); recovery of planted lineages; whole-corpus co-tablet sharing | planted lineages recovered 8/8 at 1-3 mutations; real father/son 1/8 (at 100 pairs); PE tablets 8/8 (PS ratio 1.125), headers / volumes / class signs / numeral systems: no clustering beyond the tablet; whole corpus PE co-tablet sharing 0.30 vs 0.125 (lift 2.4, z 19) vs Ur III father-son lift 1.11, OB 1.25, Ur III admin co-tablet 0.97, Linear B 1.13 | clades follow TABLETS only, and much more strongly than real kinship links names. Herd office: names resemble each other across herd tablets (p 0.035) through the |M362+X| compound: a herd-naming pattern, not shown to be lineage |
| pe7:PE-3.F | (3) Mutation clock: same find-group (publication volume = campaign, Sb accession block) closer than across? Mantel vs Sb distance | PE volume / Sb dist z -0.4 / -0.3, Mantel rho 0.077 (2/6, random strings 0.046: length confound); positives also weak (Ur III provenience 4/6, Linear B site 2/6) | no clock; test power is low even for real regional differences: upper bound only, Susa only |
| pe7:PE-4.F | Household vs tablet stock: residual after dropping signs, name-variant check, coverage, line-distance decay | residual lift 1.8 (top-15 shared signs removed) and 6.0 (25 commonest removed); only 2.9% of sharers are variants of one string; coverage like planted kin groups; NO adjacent-line excess (0.387 vs 0.377, p 0.18) while planted kin blocks give 0.76 vs 0.53 | the resemblance is a tablet-local stock of rare signs shared by partial groups, not listed as family blocks. Most economical reading: a scribe's / unit's / locality naming pool. Kinship not excluded, not needed |
| pe7:PE-5.F | Element grading by tree homoplasy (per-char RI) and its validation on Ur III father->son | Spearman per-char RI vs real father-son transmission 0.06 (p 0.51) | tree grading does NOT detect inheritance even where it is known: no PE element can be graded 'inherited' from trees |
| pe8:PE-1.F | 'Filled-in forms': 15-feature tablet skeletons (roles and number systems, names and quantities blanked) clustered by latent class EM + BIC/MDL and DP mixture; planted 6-form corpora, per-entry null corpora, column shuffle | planted 6.0 (5-7) recovered, ARI 0.72-0.82; REAL 5 clusters; null with no forms also 5; shuffle 1. LCM loses to a graded Chow-Liu tree on REAL (-0.39 nats) as on null | NO fixed forms: the cluster count is an artefact, real skeletons are graded (loops/pe8_final.txt) |
| pe8:PE-3.F | Test 1: entry number system predicted from the form (fitted without number features) vs from the final sign; planted positive control | form 0.891-0.894 bits = marginal 0.890 = permuted control; final sign 0.784; same-tablet entries 0.548; planted form gain 0.04-0.40 | FAILS: tablet layout carries no information about the number system |
| pe8:PE-4.F | Tests 2-3: same form among museum-number (Sb, NMI BK), publication and findspot neighbours (within-volume permutation); rare forms as compositions of common ones (column-permutation null) | same-form 27-35% vs 23-27% expected; header alone z 12.6; rare tablets pair-covered 8.9% vs null 14.2% | WEAK / FAILS: neighbours share headers, not forms; rare skeletons are not compositions |

CLEAN: K_bic 3, DP 4. PLANTED: K_bic 7, 6, 6, 6, 5 (mean 6.0), DP 5-8, ARI with true forms 0.72-0.82 | BIC/DP recover the planted 6 (+-1); held-out CV over-splits every corpus (12 on planted) and is not a usable form counter. Use BIC |
| pe8:PE-2.1 | Same pipeline on NULL corpora: entry count, every entry and every tablet-level field group drawn independently from real pools (layout generated per entry, no tablet coupling), 5 seeds; and on REAL with each feature column permuted (3 seeds) | NULL: K_bic 5,5,5,5,5, DP 3-5 -- the SAME count as REAL. Column-shuffled: K 1,1,1 | KILLS the count: '5 forms' is produced by how skeleton features are built from the same entries, not by templates. A corpus with no forms at all yields 5 clusters too |
| pe8:PE-3.1 | Strength of clustering instead of count: BIC gain per tablet (K_bic vs K = 1), same model, all corpora | REAL 1.62 nats/tablet; NULL 1.20-1.31 (max of 5); PLANTED 2.37-4.12 (min of 5); log-lik gain at K = 5: REAL 1.24, NULL 1.03-1.08, PLANTED 1.59-2.47 | REAL has tablet-level coherence beyond per-entry independence (above all 5 nulls) but sits far below even the weakest 6-form planted corpus: the excess over null is ~0.3-0.4 nats per tablet, planted forms give 1.1-2.9 |

Forms carry ZERO information about the number system (= marginal = permuted control); the counted good carries 0.11 bits; the tablet itself carries 0.34. Whatever ties a tablet to its system is not visible in its layout |
| pe8:PE-6.2 | Positive control for PE-5.2: same predictor on planted corpora (forms fix the entry system set) and NULL corpora | PLANTED gain over marginal 0.40, 0.26, 0.04 bits (FORM beats GOOD in 2/3); NULL gain 0.00, 0.00 | the predictor finds form -> system when it exists; its zero on REAL is a real zero (upper bound: < ~0.04 bits) |

Null: rare units' features permuted column-wise (200-1,000x); reference: 158 common tablets | rare forms fully pair-covered 0/4 (null 0.10); rare tablets 8.9% (null 14.2%, p_le 0.015); pair gain 1.68 vs 1.82 (p_le 0.005); block-composed rare tablets 22.8% vs null 9.4%, BUT common tablets 29.7% vs 10.4% | FAILS. The only composition signal (obverse block + reverse block from different forms) is stronger in common tablets: obverse and reverse are chosen separately, which argues against fixed whole-tablet templates |

| pe10:PE-10.1c | Between tablets: mean entry length vs mm of height per obverse line; null 1 = permute within entry-count bins; null 2 (strict) = permute only among tablets sharing opener sign + dominant number system + entry-count bin | >= 5 entries (143 tablets): r +0.20, p 0.02 against null 1. All intact obverses with >= 3 entries (265): r +0.03, z +0.95 against the strict null. Width vs mean length r +0.13 (unstratified) | NULL after stratification: the raw "spacious tablets have longer entries" is a genre effect (opener/number system), not a squeeze |
| pe10:PE-10.1d | POSITIVE CONTROL, Ur III year formulas (known long and short forms of the same year), 9,997 dated intact tablets in 42 years; within-year correlation of formula length (graphemes) with tablet height and with crowding (non-year lines per cm); null permutes within year (1,000) | height: r +0.066, z +6.1 (smaller tablets, shorter formulas). Crowding: r +0.031, z +3.0 (WRONG sign). Formula on an edge: +1.9 graphemes, z 22 (reverse causation: long formulas overflow onto edges). Short-form rate (below the year median) by height quartile: 25.3 / 24.7 / 25.3 / 25.4% | Calibration: even where abbreviation certainly exists, tablet size explains < 0.5% of formula-length variance and does not change the short-form rate. A squeeze effect is real but tiny; at PE sample size (10-60x fewer units) it would be invisible. Edge/position effects must not be read as squeeze (they are outcomes of length) |
| pe10:PE-10.1 | Cycle verdict | No squeeze effect in PE by position, line pressure or tablet spaciousness; the planted vertical squeeze is detectable, the horizontal one is not; Ur III shows the real effect is very small | NULL (C-grade absence: PE scribes did not visibly abbreviate when space ran out; at most < 0.4 signs on the last crowded line). Cycle 2 mines specific long<->short pairs anyway, because a few strong pairs could hide in a null average |
| pe10:PE-10.2a | Broad search: every whole-entry short string S (n >= 2, 336 types) x every contained n-gram L (n = 2-4, >= 2 occurrences, 696 types) = 233,856 pairs; context gate (opener + number system cosine >= 0.5) leaves 134,105; score (z_S - z_L)/sqrt2; FWER from the same search on Q shuffled within tablet (200 reps). Planted: 5 pairs, m = 3/5/8 S-in-Q and L-in-open placements | real max 3.96 vs null max mean 4.30, FWER threshold 5.19 (p 0.75); pairs above 4: 0 vs null mean 122. Subsequence pairs only: max 2.92 vs 4.02. No survivor. Planted: 1/5, 0/5, 1/5 pairs recovered (but the pairs-above-4 count rises to 848 / 1,113 / 3,036 against a null of 130-280) | NULL. Individual pairs are not recoverable with this score (the S term dominates: one rare string twice in Q beats any real pair); the aggregate count would catch a planted squeeze, and the real corpus is below the null |
| pe10:PE-10.2b | Pair-specific score min(z_S, -z_L) on the same pairs (S n >= 3) | real max 1.05: with a 4% Q rate a long form can never look depleted (z_L >= -1 unless n > 50) | DESIGN LIMIT: dropped. A squeeze label covering 4% of entries cannot show where a long form is avoided |

Planted (5 years, 60% of long forms on small tablets shortened): 9 survivors, 8 true. PLANTED (mirror of the Ur III plant, 5 pairs, 60% of long forms on small tablets shortened, 10 runs): 0 of 50 pairs recovered | NULL, and the PE corpus is too small for this miner: PE has 172 repeated types with few multi-sign strings (40 base-form types n >= 3) against 405 formula types in Ur III. Demoted below C (killed as stated); bare M387 may shorten some M387 + capacity-class string, untested |

The only identity found is NI -> NI. Modal partners: GRA -> OVIS, VIR -> OVIS, HIDE -> OVIS, CAP -> LANA, CYP -> FAR, VIN -> OLE / AROM, OLE -> KE / *152 | FAIL. Even two scripts of one administrative tradition, with the same commodities, are not paired sign-to-sign. CYP -> FAR (two sub-unit-measured goods) is again a class match |
| x2:X-2.3a2 | LA <-> Ur III, gold = the LB gold of the LA label (GRA -> grain words, OLE -> i3, VIN -> gesztin, VIR -> gurusz...) | hits per world: prof 0.13, joint 0.40, flood 0.00, freq 0.07 of 3.1 testable, against 0.135 by chance | FAIL (chance) |
| x2:X-2.3b | Split-half replication: 60 random halvings of LA and of PE tablets; does an LA commodity get the same PE partner in both halves? Null: 60 shuffled halvings | concordance prof 0.090 vs 0.032 (P 0.23), joint 0.118 vs 0.029 (P 0.18), flood 0.113 vs 0.034 (P 0.20); freq 0.35 vs 0.36. Per item, only GRA replicates: GRA -> M288 in 43% (prof), 75% (joint), 65% (flood) of halvings; CYP -> M036 13% (prof); everything else 0-7%. HIDE too rare to score in halves | FAIL overall. GRA -> M288 is the one pair that replicates, and it is a hub pair (M288 = PE's commonest class sign) of the kind that is stably WRONG in the control (2.2c, 3d) |
| x2:X-2.3c | Triangle: LA X -> PE Y (cycle 2); X -> LB partner (3a); Y -> Ur III partner (15 PE <-> Ur III worlds). Consistent when (LB partner, Ur III partner) is a gold LB <-> Ur III pair. Null: Y replaced by a random PE class sign, 5,000x | prof 2/14 vs 0.89 (P 0.22); joint 3/14 vs 0.45 (P 0.008); flood 3/14 vs 0.74 (P 0.024). Consistent chains: CYP -> M297 / M036 = FAR -> dabin / esza (flour); VIR -> M317 = OVIS -> udu / u8; OLIV -> M264 = BOS -> ab2; *308 / QA2 -> M379 = GRA / FAR -> esza | Looks positive, but the chains pass through WRONG links (VIR -> OVIS, OLIV -> BOS, *308 -> GRA). Tested next |
| x2:X-2.3e | Class-preserving triangle null: Y' drawn only among PE signs whose Ur III partner has the same unit class (measured: grain, flour, oil, beer, bread / counted), 20,000x | prof 2 vs 1.36 (P 0.41); joint 3 vs 2.20 (P 0.18); flood 3 vs 2.25 (P 0.23). Chain class agreement 64-79% | KILLED. The triangle consistency is fully explained by 'measured vs counted' agreement. The pipeline is coherent about unit class and about nothing finer |
| x2:X-2.3d | Control precision at the family-wise stability threshold (95th pct of per-world maxima in shuffled worlds), cycle-2 worlds | LA x PE size: prof 0.077 (91 pairs), joint 0.093 (129), flood 0.150 (167); PE size: 0.13, 0.12, 0.19; chance per pair 0.039-0.041. Stable wrong pairs dominate: OLE -> sze (oil -> barley), GRA -> ninda / kasz, OLE -> ku3, HORD -> i3, VIR -> sila4 | A family-wise LA <-> PE survivor has an expected precision of 8-15% at this size: 2-4x chance, still mostly wrong. Survivors cannot be graded above C |
| x2:X-2.final | Three cycles (tools/x2_build.py, x2_common.py, x2_fast.py, x2_cycle1-3*.py; about 40,000 alignments). Every entry in four commodity-account corpora (LA 352 tablets, PE 1,510, LB 2,478 DAMOS documents, Ur III 61,728 CDLI documents sampled) represented by script-free contexts (quantity bin, sub-unit, round ten, header / entry / trailer, total line, list position and length, commodities per tablet, designation length, quantity vs tablet median) plus internal co-occurrence. Alignments: Procrustes seeded on the shared contexts with CSLS self-learning, 30 random-rotation restarts, Gromov-Wasserstein (10 couplings), shared-profile cosine, joint SVD, similarity flooding over tablet co-occurrence; bootstrap stability. Controls: LB <-> Ur III with a gold dictionary frozen before the runs (19 commodities, 5 words), at full, PE and LA x PE sizes; LA <-> LB (same logograms by descent); shuffled corpora on both sides; per-label and family-wise shuffled nulls; split halves; triangle LA -> PE -> Ur III vs LA -> LB, with a class-preserving null | Decisive control: the requested unsupervised-MT alignments (Procrustes, random restarts, GW) do not recover LB <-> Ur III commodities at any size (MRR at or below the permutation null). The best no-learning profile match and flooding recover them only at PE size (flooding P 0.049 vs shuffled worlds) and not at LA x PE size (P 0.15-0.46). Hits are class-level (FAR -> flour, OVIS -> sheep); stable errors swap oil and barley, bronze and beer, men and lambs. LA <-> LB identity: 1 of 7 (NI). Stability does not predict correctness (control precision of family-wise survivors 8-19% vs 4% chance). LA <-> PE: family-wise survivors HIDE -> M288 and GRA -> M288; only GRA -> M288 replicates in split halves (43-75%); CYP pairs with the capacity class signs M297 / M036 / M379; triangle consistency (P 0.008-0.02) dies under a class-preserving null (P 0.18-0.41). Total word aligns across scripts (built in); LA heading words (A-DU, *516, TE) meet PE openers (M157, M327+M342) only at slot level | **No LA <-> PE sign-level alignment survives the controls.** The two scripts do not translate each other from numbers and context: the method fails on the pair where the answer is known (LB <-> Ur III) at the size that matters, and on two scripts of one tradition (LA <-> LB). What crosses scripts is one bit per commodity, measured-in-sub-units vs counted, and the slot frame (heading, entry, total). Not cracked |

| pe11:PE-11.F1 | Clock from the animals, calibration: per-tablet month search (annealed Gibbs + hard EM) under a yearly loop vs open drift with equal parameters; label-free loop excess vs copula / shuffle / random-walk surrogates; real control = 8,255 Ur III Drehem livestock tablets with known months; planted PE-shaped herd office; synthetic rings | Drehem: month = 1.0% of variance; ordering barely above chance (0.29-0.32 vs 0.28-0.30 within +-1 month); label-free D below the copula null. Simulated office never detected. Detection needs a season carrying ~40% of feature variance | method can order but cannot certify an order without dates; real herd seasons are ~40x too weak |
| pe11:PE-11.F2 | PE film: 638 tablets, ALL / HERD / GRAIN sets vs 20 surrogates each (60 for ALL/HERD); 1,500 random feature subsets with FWER from 8 copula replicates of the whole search; top 1% held out; TYPELOOP control (timeless three-commodity mixtures) | ALL z +1.4, HERD z +2.4 (p 0.03-0.05), GRAIN none; TYPELOOP z +2.0, Drehem z -2.0; search FWER p 0.56; 0 of 15 held-out survivors; inferred months lumpy (0-137 tablets) and track tablet type | NULL: no time order; the weak loop is a tour of commodity types (B: no strong yearly cycle) |
| pe11:PE-11.F3 | Signs switching at inferred season boundaries (127 non-feature signs, 3-month arc share, global and within-commodity-cluster permutation nulls, FWER); planted season header | planted header z 18 (FWER 0.001) with a recovered order, invisible with an unrecovered one; real best M338 FWER 0.064, header M388 0.085 | NULL; C hint M338 / M388 (would support: one-season clustering in a dated or stratified context; would kill: an even spread there) |
| pe11:PE-11.1a | REAL POSITIVE CONTROL, Ur III Drehem livestock tablets with known month (8,255 tablets; 15 features: head shares of lamb, kid, ewe, sheep, goat, nanny, ox, cow, calf, gazelle, gazelle fawn, donkey, grain-fed share, log total, log lines). Ground truth: variance explained by month | month explains 1.0% of feature variance (gazelle fawns 5.6%, log total 2.5%, lambs 1.1%); fawns peak in months 12-1, totals in months 8-9, ewes in months 4-8 | real herd seasonality exists but is a small fraction of tablet-to-tablet variance |
| pe11:PE-11.1b | Blind search on Drehem at PE size (n 638, 3 draws) and n 2000 (3 draws); D and R2c vs 10 copula / shuffle / random-walk surrogates; recovery vs permuted months | n 638: recovery 0.318 / 0.268 / 0.303 vs null 95% 0.315 / 0.298 / 0.304 (1 of 3 passes); n 2000: 0.302 / 0.293 / 0.289 vs null 95% 0.287 / 0.280 / 0.289 (2-3 of 3 barely pass). Label-free: D real -0.012 to -0.002, copula D mean +0.001 to +0.002 (real is LOWER than null); R2c real 0.19 > copula max 0.18 (non-Gaussian clustering, not time) | a real yearly cycle is recovered only marginally (+2-3 points over chance) and ONLY when the true months are known; the label-free ring statistic does not see it at all |
| pe11:PE-11.1c | SIMULATED HERD OFFICE built from PE's own 638 tablets (same entry counts, same sign mix per tablet smoothed 50% to corpus, values resampled from each sign's PE values): lambing (M263 share x up to 1+4a, peak month 2), culling (M346, month 8), harvest stock (M297 amounts, jump month 5, linear decline), herd size; amplitudes a 0.25-16, seasonal fraction f 1 or 0.5 | month R2 0.02-0.07 (entry sampling noise caps it); recovery 0.28-0.32 vs null 95% 0.29-0.30 (passes from a = 2 upward); D vs copula p 0.27-0.91, never significant; R2c vs copula p 0.09-0.45 | planted seasons in PE-shaped data are invisible to the label-free search at every amplitude; PE's sparse entries (median ~5 per tablet) cannot carry a strong season signal |
| pe11:PE-11.1d | Drehem month bundles (306 frames = mean of all tablets of one year+month, >= 5 tablets; dates used only to build bundles and to score) and Drehem 4 most seasonal features on single tablets (n 2000) | bundles: month R2 9.3%, recovery 0.382 vs null 95% 0.327 (passes clearly), but D p 0.64, R2c p 1.0 vs copula; 4 features: recovery 0.323 vs 0.295, D p 0.82 | even a 9% season, correctly ordered by the search, does not produce a label-free ring signature |
| pe11:PE-11.1e | Detection threshold: pure synthetic ring (4 features, phases 0/3/6/9 months) plus noise, season share R2s 0.05-0.8, n 638, D and R2c vs 10 copula surrogates | recovery passes at every level (0.41 at R2s 0.05, 0.72 at 0.4, 1.00 at 0.8); label-free D beats all 10 copula surrogates at R2s 0.4 (0.020 vs max 0.017) and 0.8 (0.071 vs 0.029), not reliably below (0.2: p 0.45; 0.1: p 0.27; 0.05: p 0.09 = noise); R2c beats copula only at 0.4-0.8 | the blind ring statistic needs a season explaining roughly >= 40% of the variance of the chosen features |
| pe11:PE-11.1 | Cycle verdict | The film search has power to ORDER tablets when a season exists, but without known dates nothing can certify the order: the label-free statistics need a season ~40x stronger (share of variance) than the real Drehem livestock season (1%, 9% even in month bundles) | CALIBRATION: any PE result from this method can only kill a very strong season (>= ~40% of feature variance); a PE null is NOT evidence against a yearly herd cycle. Cycle 2 runs PE anyway with the full nulls and the massive random feature search |
| pe11:PE-11.2a | Fixed sets, real D vs 20 copula, 20 shuffle, 20 random-walk surrogates | ALL: D +0.0006 vs copula -0.0023 (max -0.0001, p 0.05), shuffle p 0.05, random walk p 0.14. HERD: -0.0025 vs copula -0.0082 (p 0.05), shuffle p 0.05, random walk p 0.05. GRAIN: -0.025 vs copula -0.024 (p 0.52), shuffle p 1.0, random walk p 0.57 | small loop excess in ALL and HERD; none in GRAIN (no harvest ring in the capacity amounts) |
| pe11:PE-11.2b | Massive random guessing: 1,500 random feature subsets (3-6 of 24) on random half A (319 tablets); family-wise null = the whole search on 8 copula surrogates of half A; PLANTED: strong ring (half the variance) added to 4 features (M218, M367, M001 shares, fraction share) | real max D 0.070 vs null replicate maxima 0.038-0.154 (FWER p 0.56); 18 real subsets above the pooled null 99th centile (15 expected). Planted: subsets holding all 4 planted features D +0.030 vs -0.026 for 0 of 4, but planted max 0.057 is below 3 of 8 null maxima | NULL. Weak control: heavy-tailed 3-feature subsets make the family-wise null so wide that even the planted ring does not clear it; the search can rank but not certify |
| pe11:PE-11.2c | Held-out: top 15 real subsets (1%) re-scored on half B against 30 copula surrogates each, Bonferroni x15 | 0 of 15 survive (best raw p 0.065 for capacity share + mean log entry + share of 1s + long-string share; Bonferroni 0.97); the top subset (M297 share, capacity share, log counted total; D_A 0.070) gives D_B -0.002, p 0.16 | NULL: no subset's loop replicates on unseen tablets |
| pe11:PE-11.2d | Is the ALL/HERD excess a clock? Same statistic, 60 copula surrogates each, against (i) the Drehem real control (n 638, a true yearly cycle) and (ii) TYPELOOP: synthetic tablets that are static two-way mixtures of three PE commodity pools (A-B, B-C, C-A), i.e. a loop in commodity space with NO time | PE ALL z +1.4 (p 0.049), PE HERD z +2.4 (p 0.033); TYPELOOP (no time) z +2.0 (p 0.066); Drehem (real seasons) z -2.0 (p 0.98) | the PE excess is exactly the size a timeless commodity loop produces, and real seasonal herd accounts do NOT produce it: the loop is not a clock |
| pe11:PE-11.2e | Anatomy of the inferred PE "year" (ALL order) | phase occupancy 137, 119, 31, 35, 40, 31, 12, 0, 1, 14, 99, 119 (very uneven: a real calendar should be near-uniform); the loop runs through commodity types: M066 share (range 5.3 sd, peak phase 8), capacity share and M297 (8-9), log counted total and mean entry size (1), M218 share and share of 1s (5), M371 (4) | the "film" is a tour of tablet types (capacity tablets -> big counted tablets -> M218/M371 small-count lists), not a season |
| pe11:PE-11.2 | Cycle verdict | Some PE feature sets close a slightly better loop than Gaussian surrogates, but (1) the same excess appears in a timeless commodity-mixture control, (2) real Ur III seasonal data do not show it, (3) nothing survives held-out re-test, (4) phase occupancy is lumpy | NULL. No time order beats the null; with cycle 1 calibration, this kills only a very strong season (>= ~40% of feature variance). Grade B for "no strong yearly cycle in tablet composition"; C for anything weaker (untestable) |
| pe11:PE-11.3a | POSITIVE CONTROL, recovered order: planted ring (half the variance of 4 features) searched on those 4 features; planted header 'HSPR' on half the tablets of true months 2-4 (n 83) | order recovery 0.90 (within +-1 month); HSPR arc share 0.87 vs 0.33 by chance, rank 1 of 128 under both nulls: null A z 18.0, FWER p 0.001; null B z 4.8, FWER p 0.07 (the strict null partly absorbs it because the same 4 features define both phase and strata) | the switch test finds a season word when the order is right |
| pe11:PE-11.3b | NEGATIVE CALIBRATION, unrecovered order: the same plant with the order searched on all 24 features (recovery 0.31), and the cycle-1 simulated office (a = 4, recovery 0.32) | HSPR not in the top 4 (ring) / z 1.3, FWER p 1.0 (simulation); top signs are chance (best FWER p 0.07-0.34) | the test is only as good as the order: with a wrong order, a real season word is invisible |
| pe11:PE-11.3c | Real PE order (ALL features, cycle 2) | null A best: M036 (non-final), n 20, arc 0.90, z 2.9, FWER p 0.17; M051, M340, M266 all in the same arc (months 10-0, which is the capacity-tablet end of the loop). Null B best: M338 (n 30) z 3.4, FWER p 0.064; header M388 (n 9) z 3.4, FWER p 0.085 | NULL: no sign switches at an inferred boundary beyond chance; the null-A leaders sit at the capacity-tablet end of the commodity loop (M036 is the capacity-tied sign from test b), i.e. they track tablet type, not time |
| pe11:PE-11.3 | Cycle verdict | The switch test is calibrated (planted season header found at z 18 when the order is right) but the PE order is not a clock (cycle 2), and no sign passes on it | NULL. No month, season or seasonal-operation sign is predicted. Grade C hint only, to be killed or confirmed by any future dated-context test: M338 and header M388 (FWER p 0.06-0.09 under the strict null) |
| pe9:PE-1.F | 'Names drawn from a bag': held-out likelihood race (5-fold CV) of a language model, a tablet-topic cache (POLYA), inexhaustible bag, depleting bag (K types x c copies, refilled), stamps; controls: middles shuffled across tablets, planted bag/POLYA/LM corpora, Ur III admin and Linear B names | PE: POLYA 8.425 bits/sign < STAMP 8.464 < BAG_WR 8.501 < LM 8.845 = BAG_WOR 8.846; gain 0.42 vs shuffled 0.07-0.10; planted bag recovered (K 12, c 2 exact); Ur III no tablet gain (0.01) | tablet-topic model wins; a depleting bag of counters gains nothing (loops/pe9_cycle1.txt) |
| pe9:PE-2.F | Use profile: re-use chance after 1, 2, >= 3 uses on a tablet (x LM); planted 2-copy bag must collapse | PE 2.6, 2.9, 4.6; shuffled ~1; planted bag 8.6 then 0.5-0.7; Ur III 1.2, 1.0, 1.1; Linear B 1.3, 1.6, 1.7 | rich-get-richer, not depletion |
| pe9:PE-3.F | ABC on 1,000,000 simulated PE corpora (+100,000 Ur III, +100,000 Linear B); planted and 300 leave-one-out recoveries | PE: POLYA 0.45, STAMP 0.26, BAG_WR 0.20, BAG_WOR 0.09 (1 copy 0.00, 2 copies 0.01), LM 0.00; refill per tablet 0.83; pool ~10-50 types giving ~40% of signs. Shuffled -> LM; Ur III -> LM 0.65; Linear B -> STAMP 0.71 (control fails); planted bag 0.98-0.996 with c 2, K ~10; LOO acc 0.73, log K r 0.94 | per-tablet inexhaustible pool; 1-2-copy counters excluded. Real Linear B names also prefer a pool, so a pool does not mean 'not names'. No model reproduces PE's hapax-rich inventory (0.35 vs 0.22-0.29) (loops/pe9_cycle2.txt) |
| pe9:PE-4.F | Depleted vs inexhaustible signs (within-tablet pair excess vs 1,000 shuffles; 20 shuffled corpora as false-positive control) and 4 pre-registered predictions on 52 reserved tablets | depleted 0/90 (null 0.05); inexhaustible 30/90 (null 4.3, max 10): M370, M376, M124, M388, M145, M203, M001, M054, M136. Reserved: P1 PASS (f2 2.2, P(f2<1) 0), P2 PASS (0.40 bits vs shuffled max 0.13), P3 FAIL (p 0.15), P4 FAIL (re-use rate separates, 3+ statistic does not) | no sign behaves as a used-up token; the boost predicts unseen tablets; per-sign classes not replicated at 52 tablets (loops/pe9_cycle3.txt, pe9_final.txt) |
| pe9:PE-1.1 | Held-out bits/sign, five model families, vs PE middles shuffled across tablets and a planted LM corpus (which measure the gain a cache gets from smoothing alone) | PE: LM 8.845, POLYA 8.425, STAMP 8.464, BAG_WR 8.501, BAG_WOR 8.846. Gain over LM: POLYA 0.42 (shuffled 0.07-0.10; planted LM 0.06); BAG_WOR 0.00 (it fits as eps 0.9, K 2: the bag is switched off) | the tablet effect is real (restates pe7) and is best described by a TOPIC/CACHE model, not by a depleting bag. Every finite-copy bag loses |
| pe9:PE-2.1 | Recovery control: planted corpora scored by the same race | PLANT_BAG (2 seeds): BAG_WOR wins (6.96 / 6.78 vs POLYA 7.64 / 7.57), fitted K 12, c 2 = the planted values exactly (eps 0.5 vs 0.15 planted); PLANT_POLYA: POLYA wins by 0.25-0.6 bits; PLANT_LM: all within 0.06 | the race CAN find a depleting bag and its size when one is there. Its silence on PE is informative |
| pe9:PE-3.1 | Language controls: Ur III admin names and Linear B names | UR3: tablet gain 0.012 bits (POLYA = BAG_WR 3.970 vs LM 3.982), i.e. no tablet effect; LINB: POLYA wins by 0.18, BAG_WOR = LM | both controls prefer the language-like model (UR3) or the cache (LINB); neither prefers a depleting bag. PASS |
| pe9:PE-4.1 | Use profile (cycle 1b): P(sign) = LM x r, r = f1, f2, f3 after 1, 2, >= 3 earlier uses on the tablet, fw inside the same string; 200 tablet bootstraps | PE f = 2.62, 2.87, 4.61 (90% CI f1 2.2-2.9, f2 2.0-3.4, f3 3.1-5.8), fw 1.09; shuffled ~1 (0.75-1.19); planted BAG c 2: f1 8.6 then f2 0.5-0.7 (collapse); planted POLYA 12, 16, 28-38; UR3 1.2, 1.0, 1.1; LINB 1.3, 1.6, 1.65 | NO depletion: a sign used once on a tablet becomes MORE likely to be used again, and more so after each use. Inside one string re-use is neither boosted nor suppressed (fw 1.09). The bag-of-counters reading is killed for c = 1 and c = 2; c >= 3 cannot be ruled out but f3 > f2 argues against it |
| pe9:PE-1.2 | ABC model choice on PE (1,000 of 1,000,000 accepted) vs PE middles shuffled across tablets (3 seeds) | PE: POLYA 0.45, STAMP 0.26, BAG_WR 0.20, BAG_WOR 0.09, LM 0.00. Shuffled: LM 0.67-0.69, POLYA 0.00 | the language-like model with no tablet effect is excluded (0 of 1,000); the shuffle kills the tablet models. Among tablet models the topic/cache wins; the depleting bag is the least supported |
| pe9:PE-2.2 | Bag size and refill rule (accepted sims) | BAG_WOR c: 1 copy 0.00, 2 copies 0.01, 3 copies 0.27, 5 0.24, 10 0.48; K (types per bag) 9-41 (median 26) with eps 0.12-0.87 (median 0.6); BAG_WR K 9-54 (median 29); session S = 1 tablet 0.83, 2 0.15, 4 0.03, 8 0.004 | IF a bag, it is refilled for every tablet, holds roughly 10-50 sign types, supplies only about 40% of the signs, and each type is effectively inexhaustible (>= 3 copies). One- and two-copy counters are excluded |
| pe9:PE-3.2 | Recovery controls: planted BAG_WOR (K 12, c 2, eps 0.15) and POLYA (theta 3) corpora; 300 leave-one-out pseudo-observed sims from the table | planted bag: BAG_WOR 0.98 / 0.996, c = 2 at 0.90 / 0.96, K 10.4 / 9.6 (90% 3-19), S = 1 at 0.99; planted POLYA: POLYA 0.97-0.996, theta 2.7 / 3.25 (true 3); planted LM: LM 0.64 / 0.85. LOO: model accuracy 0.73 (LM 59/59, POLYA 49/54, STAMP 46/57, BAG_WR 47/67, BAG_WOR 18/63, mostly confused with BAG_WR/LM when eps is high); log K recovery r 0.94, c recovery r 0.90 | the machinery recovers a depleting bag and its parameters when one is planted. BAG_WR vs BAG_WOR at high eps is weakly separable, so 'c >= 3' is an upper-level statement, not a precise count |
| pe9:PE-4.2 | Language controls on their own tables | Ur III admin names: LM 0.65 (POLYA 0.00; bag models only at eps 0.95). Linear B names: STAMP 0.71, BAG_WR 0.21, POLYA 0.05, LM 0.00, session 4-8 tablets | Ur III PASSES (prefers the language model). Linear B FAILS the intended control: its names also prefer a tablet-pool model (stamps), so 'prefers a bag' is NOT diagnostic of non-linguistic names -- real names on themed tablets do it too. PE differs from Linear B in session length (1 tablet vs 4-8) and in strength (cycle 1: f 2.6-4.6 vs 1.3-1.6) |
| pe9:PE-5.2 | Posterior-predictive check (observed vs 5-95% of accepted sims) | PE outside on 3 of 11: types/token 0.127 vs 0.101-0.113, hapax share 0.353 vs 0.219-0.285, re-use rate 0.237 vs 0.158-0.232. Ur III also fails hapax (0.226 vs 0.162-0.206); planted corpora pass | no model in the family reproduces PE's surplus of one-off signs; all models also under-produce re-use. The sign inventory is richer in hapaxes than any frequency-weighted generator (bag or language) gives |
| pe9:PE-1.3 | Per sign (90 signs on >= 6 fit-set tablets): within-tablet string pairs that share the sign vs 1,000 shuffles of middles across tablets; 'depleted' z <= -2, 'inexhaustible' z >= +2. False-positive control: 20 shuffled corpora run as if observed | depleted: 0 of 90 (shuffled: mean 0.05, max 1); inexhaustible: 30 of 90 (shuffled: mean 4.3, max 10). Top: M370 (76 pairs vs 8.4 expected), M376 (18 vs 1.1), M124 (206 vs 47), M388 (243 vs 94), M145, M203, M001, M054, M136, M210, |M036+1(N30D)|, M002 | NO sign behaves like a depleted counter (upper bound: < 3/90 signs at this power). About a third of the common signs are re-used on a tablet far beyond chance. The top of the list overlaps pe7's tablet-shared signs (M388, M124, M370, M054, M001, M376): same effect, new framing |
| pe9:PE-2.3 | P1: use profile on the 52 reserved tablets (base from fit set), 200 bootstraps | f1 3.12 (90% 2.6-4.1), f2 2.18 (1.3-3.4), f3 14.2 (5.1-22.8), fw 1.22; P(f2 < 1) = 0.00 | PASS: second and third uses are boosted, not suppressed; no depletion at <= 2 copies in unseen tablets |
| pe9:PE-3.3 | P2: held-out gain of POLYA (theta 1,000 fitted on fit set) over LM on reserved tablets vs reserved middles shuffled across reserved tablets (100x) | gain 0.397 bits/sign vs shuffled mean 0.066, max 0.128 (p < 0.01) | PASS: the tablet-topic effect predicts unseen tablets at the size measured in cycle 1 (0.42) |
| pe9:PE-4.3 | P3: fit-set 'inexhaustible' signs vs others, reserved pair excess per tablet-occurrence, label permutation (5,000x) | only 4 inexhaustible and 21 other signs occur on >= 2 reserved tablets; excess 0.32 vs 0.15 (p 0.15) | FAIL (underpowered: 52 tablets). Direction as predicted; the per-sign classes are not replicated |
| pe9:PE-5.3 | P4: reserved P(>= 3 strings given >= 2) and re-use rate vs 5-95% posterior-predictive bands (ABC-accepted parameters, and best BAG_WOR c <= 2 parameters) simulated on the reserved structure | re-use rate 0.212: inside ABC band (0.120-0.219), OUTSIDE bag c <= 2 band (0.091-0.142). P(>= 3 given 2) 0.255: inside ABC band (0.109-0.277) and inside the bag band (0.116-0.270). Within-tablet sharing 0.181 above the ABC band (0.044-0.148) | FAIL as pre-registered (one of two statistics does not separate the models at 52 tablets). The re-use rate alone excludes the two-copy bag on unseen tablets. Unseen tablets share even more than the model expects |
| pe9:PE-5.F | Pre-registered predictions on 52 reserved tablets | P1 PASS (f2 2.2, P(f2<1) 0), P2 PASS (0.40 bits vs shuffled max 0.13), P3 FAIL (p 0.15, 4 signs), P4 FAIL (re-use rate separates, 3+ statistic does not) | the main prediction (boost, not depletion) holds out of sample; the fine-grained ones are underpowered |
| r2:R-2.final | ~8.9 million programs on 21 corpus x mode runs: 3 real scripts, 13 nulls, 3 planted generators (grille, 2 ledgers), 2 natural languages (Latin, Linear B); layout-given rerun on 11 | Planted generators recovered 3/3, the mechanism readable in the winning program (`PL 0 LI if tab1` = grille row from the line number; `... AB ... tab2` = commodity cycle read from the line above): +24 to +97 mbit/token on held-out text. Linear B: no gain (0/30). Latin: gain only from line-end layout; with layout given <= 2 mbit/token. Real, layout given, vs KN + document cache: LA +28 bits net (p 0.14), PE +20 (p 0.28), Voynich -50 on KN alone (Markov resynthesis +42). Shuffles always beat KN (+97 to +3,900 bits) via the token skeleton | No program beats the baselines on held-out data beyond its nulls in any script. What the search finds in LA and PE is 'copy what the line or entry above had' (logograms, entry-initial signs, word length): within-document repetition, which a cache model already explains (known in kind: list entries repeat). Voynich glyph order is not reproduced by any short procedure better than a 3-glyph Markov chain; Markov-resynthesised text gains slightly more from the search than the real text. Grades: 'no short generator (<= 15 ops, these primitives) for Voynich' A (negative, calibrated: planted grille found at 24.7 mbit/token, real <= 0); 'LA/PE entries copy from the line above' B (known kind); procedural origin of any of the three texts: not supported. Not cracked |

| pe13:PE-13.F1 | 'The scribe's memory has a shape': 1,201 random memory kernels (flat, exponential, power, step; floor, gap-1 dip, column and reverse factors; entry and raw-line clocks), 5-fold held-out by tablet, plus MLE; null 20 within-tablet line shuffles and 5 topic-model surrogates; 11 planted mechanisms on PE's skeleton; controls Ur III, Linear B, proto-cuneiform admin and lexical | PE gain over flat 3.6 millibits/sign (shuffles max 1.0; controls 4.4-257); shape = flat + dip at gap 1 (R(1) 0.91, R(2) 1.13), robust without headers, class signs or reverse; all controls peak at gap 1; planted topic / priming / dip / reset / copy / mixture recovered | Grade B: no fading running memory; the PE kernel is a flat tablet topic with an adjacent-entry dip, unlike every control |

| pe13:PE-13.F3 | Per-sign gap profiles (178 signs) and parity z, 20 + 10 shuffled corpora as false-positive control, split halves; 3,000 random tablet groups with FWER and held-out re-test (planted alternation found at t 12) | primed signs 0 (chance 1; planted 85); M288, M346 avoid the next entry and recur every second (both halves); herd tablets cycle fixed runs (P008295); no tablet group survives (best t 2.3 vs 3.1) | Grade B: alternation carried by M288 / M346; no sign carries priming |
| pe13:PE-13.F4 | Canonical order: precedence consistency and held-out order prediction (Ur III, lexical, planted orders at 3 strengths as controls); 4,000 random sign sets with FWER and held-out re-test | held-out accuracy 0.555 vs 0.527 (z 3.6), like proto-cuneiform admin (0.532), far below Ur III 0.637 and lexical 0.627; random set search 0 survivors (underpowered: planted only borderline) | Grade B: weak shared order of entries; grade C: loose habitual sorting by type, not a memorised list |

ARCH_ADM: 4.4 (<= 0.14), tau 11, copy 0.093 vs 0.057 | A COPIED list is NOT flat: it inherits the strong local order of its source (tau 1.4) and shows a large cross-tablet copy excess. Best interpretable fit: flat with a near-total dip at d=1 (rho 0.05) and reverse lines leaning MORE on the obverse than on their own face (g2 2.26; shuffles 0.59-1.63) | PE has the weakest local order of all five corpora (3.6 vs 4.4-257 millibits) and the kernel that fits is flat + a refractory dip at the adjacent line, not a decaying memory. (decay, z 2.5), Linear B 1.11 1.06 1.01 (flat-decay), proto-cuneiform admin 1.20 1.09 1.11 (decay), lexical 1.86 1.38 1.17 (strong decay) | PE has a small zig-zag (dips at gaps 1, 3, 5; peaks at 2, 4 and 8) that no control corpus shows: every real control decays from gap 1. Box cannot tell PE sign order from random within lines. |

Administrative corpora teach LA and PE structurally (PC->LA +0.33, PE->LA +0.27, identity part 0); Voynich learns from none of 9 corpora. LA<->LB syllabic identity +0.12/+0.13 (replication); LA<->Greek 0.00-0.03; Akkadian->LA 0.01 once NUM is unshared | Who teaches whom is genre, not kinship. No identity link among LA, PE, VOY is testable without shared labels, and none is found elsewhere beyond what transliterations build in. Not cracked |

Identity transfer can only be asked where conventions share labels |

| pe15:PE-15.1 | THE ADMINISTRATION IS A FOOD WEB, structure: consumer (name string or name sign) x resource (class sign \| number system; header) bipartite webs; NODF and Barber Q vs curveball fixed-degree nulls, Bluethgen H2' vs event re-pairing and within-tablet shuffles; controls Ur III (7 sites; Drehem with receiving official), Linear B KN+PY, planted modular / no-module webs | PE NODF 40-65, z -0.3 to +0.7 (controls anti-nested, z -1.3 to -5.1; planted modular +5.6); Q z +0.2 (signs), +2.0 (strings), +4 (header webs) vs Ur III +7.5/+13, LB +8-9, planted +114; H2' PE 0.29-0.51, z +27-34 (+14-16.5 within tablet) | Grade B: PE webs are nested and compartmented only as far as their degrees force; unlike Ur III and LB they have no binary compartments. The structure is in the weights (H2'), not in which links exist |
| pe15:PE-15.2 | Hidden offices: bipartite degree-corrected SBM, annealed MCMC, restarts, blocks chosen by whole-tablet hold-out; NMI of blocks with known offices in the controls | planted NMI 0.94 (null 0.00); Ur III site 0.15 (perm max 0.06), Drehem official 0.23 (0.13), LB series 0.38 (0.13). | pe16:PE-16.1 | HUNGER IS THE RULER, positive control: biology-sized needs (energy by class, grain energy and density, animal feeding) x latent period per tablet {1, 30, 360} / unit; Ur III '-ta' lines (2,750), sila known; shuffled amounts; PE-sized subsets; 600 random rulers | blind 0.83 l per sila [0.42-1.60] (truth ~1); daily vs monthly tablets right 97-100%; shuffled -3,600 log units; subsets cover 8/12, alias x12 4/12; random rulers fit as well (rank 217/600) | PASS for scale; no selection by fit; month <-> year aliasing |
| pe16:PE-16.2 | Same on PE: two-line record capacity lines (108) and all capacity entries (1,373, head counts unknown, k-model calibrated on Ur III totals: 2.5x low); nulls: shuffled, same-code random counts, 4 value sets | records: N39C = 3.2 adult-man-days = 4.6 l [1.5-13.7]; same-code null gives 2.9-7.2 l (scale = notation range); all entries 0.56 l raw, ~1.4 l corrected, x10 across value sets | C: a scale lattice, not a point |
| pe16:PE-16.3 | Planted PE-shaped records; who eats; 2,000 random rulers on PE; vessel check vs measured bevelled-rim bowls (0.5-1.2 l) | plants recovered when periods mix (7/10 within 1.6x); record companions 0.2-0.67 of the main line; biology ranks 1,125/2,000; bowl-sized unit only at the /30 point (N30C 0.62 l, N24 1.85 l = an adult day), but a chance hit is 43% likely | C, weak: N30C ~ one bowl, N24 ~ one adult-day; would support: Susa III vessels near 0.6 and 1.9 l (1:3); would kill: PE ration vessels far from 0.6 l |
| pe16:PE-16.1a | Ur III, blind: posterior of u' and litres per sila; per-tablet period at the posterior mode (160 physiology draws) | u' 0.59 man-days per sila (truth 0.5); litres per sila 0.83 [90%: 0.42-1.60], truth about 1. Period right on 134/137 hired-wage tablets (daily), 118/118 men's 60-sila tablets (monthly), 78/78 women's, 77/81 children's, 172/172 sheep (daily), 93/94 cattle (daily) | PASS. Biology alone recovers the litre size of the sila within a factor 1.2 and sorts daily wages and fodder from monthly rations |
| pe16:PE-16.1b | Single-period subset: monthly human rations only (1,534 lines, value >= 10 sila) | u' 0.88, litres 1.23 [0.63-2.36]; all posterior mass on the monthly solution | PASS, but the period is fixed by a minority of odd entries (an all-daily solution has a different set of alternative periods), not by a true single-period dataset |
| pe16:PE-16.1c | NULL: amounts shuffled across all entries (labels and tablets kept), 5 shuffles | log evidence real - shuffled = +3,623 to +3,705; shuffled data put u' at 6-9 man-days per sila (wrong by 12-18x) | the ruler reads the link between who and how much, not the bare distribution of numbers |
| pe16:PE-16.1d | CALIBRATION at PE size: 12 random tablet subsets of ~130 lines (size of the PE record set) and 12 of ~1,300 (size of all PE capacity entries) | 130 lines: the 90% interval covers 1 litre in 8/12; u' within 0.25-1 in 6/12; 4/12 alias to ~12x (yearly reading). 1,300 lines: 8/12 cover; 4/12 alias by ~10-12x | the stated intervals are too narrow (about 67% real coverage for a nominal 90%), and the period can alias by x12 (month <-> year) even at 1,300 lines. Any PE result must be read on the lattice {u'/30, u', 12u'} |
| pe16:PE-16.1e | RANDOM RULERS: 600 nonsense yardsticks (6 random class needs, log-uniform 0.05-10 l/day, and a random calendar {1, a, b}) scored by marginal likelihood on half the Ur III tablets, top 10 re-tested on the other half; real biology + {1, 30, 360} as the candidate | real biology ranks 217th of 600 on half A (log evidence -1,631 mean of 4 draws, range -1,564 to -1,659; random rulers median -1,673, 95th pct -1,487, best -1,394). The 10 best random rulers beat real biology on the held-out half too (9/10; -1,524 to -1,782 vs -1,731). They use short calendars ({1, 4-12, 5-34} days) and needs that copy the Ur III clusters | FAIL as a selection test: fit quality does not single out the true yardstick; free nonsense rulers fit better because they have more freedom. What biology gives is the absolute scale (PE-16.1a), not a better fit. So on PE, a good or bad fit cannot confirm or kill the ruler; only its litre value can be checked outside |
| pe16:PE-16.2a | CALIBRATION of the k-model: Ur III per-person amounts x the line's head count (2,335 group totals with known k), k unknown to the model | u' 0.21 man-days per sila (truth 0.5); litres per sila 0.32 [0.16-1.55]; without k: 0.17 l | k-model BIASED LOW by about 2.5x (truth is just inside the 90% band). Group totals let the model shrink the unit and invent recipients. S results must be read as a lower bound |
| pe16:PE-16.2b | PE records (R), 200 physiology draws | u' 3.2 man-days per N39C [90%: 1.3-7.1]; modes 2.5, 4.2, 6.2, 1.5; litres per N39C 4.6 [1.5-13.7]; tablets split between the 30- and 360-day period (44 vs 64 lines at the main mode) | a definite scale, but see the nulls |
| pe16:PE-16.2c | NULL 1: R amounts shuffled across lines (6 shuffles) | log evidence -230 to -244 against -148 real; litres per N39C scatter 0.6-8.2 | real records are far more coherent than shuffled ones (much of this is the trivial repetition of the same amount inside a record tablet) |
| pe16:PE-16.2d | NULL 2: same numeral codes per line, counts redrawn at random from the counts seen for that code (6 draws) | log evidence -152 to -168 (real -148, better by 4-20); litres per N39C 2.9-7.2 (real 4.6) | the litre value is set by WHICH units the scribes used, not by the counts. The counts add a modest fit. The scale is a property of the notation's working range |
| pe16:PE-16.2e | 4 alternative value sets (N24 = 8; N01 = 144; N30D = 3; N39B = 60 / N01 = 300) | litres per N39C 3.4-3.5 (three sets), 1.9 (alt_d); 90% bands 0.1-12.7 | robust to the value set within a factor 2.5 |
| pe16:PE-16.2f | All PE capacity entries (S) with the k-model; shuffled and digit nulls; alternative value sets | u' 0.40 man-days per N39C (posterior collapsed to one grid point: overconfident); litres per N39C 0.56 [0.28-1.09]; after the 2.5x bias of PE-16.2a about 1.4 l. Shuffled: log evidence -3,047 to -3,094 vs -2,662 real; digit null: -2,730 to -2,761 (real better by 68-100) with u' scattered 0.08-6.2. Alternative value sets: 0.38, 0.62, 1.6, 3.9 l | the full capacity corpus carries real structure beyond its notation (digit null loses by 68+ log units), but its scale is biased by the unknown head counts and moves x10 with the value set. Its point (0.6-1.4 l per N39C) sits between the R lattice points 0.15 and 4.6 l: R and S disagree by x3-8 |
| pe16:PE-16.3a | PLANTED: the 14 PE record tablets with amounts replaced by model draws at known u' = 3 man-days per N39C, rounded to whole N39C; 10 plants with mixed periods (30 or 360 per tablet), 10 with all 30 | mixed: 9/10 intervals cover 3, 7/10 medians within 1.6x; interval widths 1.3-2.2x in 7 plants, 30-39x in 3. All-monthly: 9/10 cover, but widths 12-1,500x (degenerate, as expected) | the record set can pin u' only when its tablets mix periods. Real R has a width of 5.5x, like the mixed plants: the fitted solution relies on a x12 spread between tablets (amounts 4-12 vs 60-300 N39C) being a period difference. It may instead be a difference of commodity or recipient. Not testable here |
| pe16:PE-16.3b | WHO EATS at the three posterior modes (u' 2.5, 4.2, 6.2): per-tablet period, then man-day fraction per day for each line | Unstable between modes except P009000: period 30 with p 1.0; main 1(N24) = adult-man size at u' 2.5, companion 1(N30C) = child or sheep size (1/3 of main). Capacity companions are smaller than their main lines on all 3 tablets where both are capacity (P009000 4 vs 12; P008403 48 vs 72; P009123 12 vs 48-60). Ratios 0.33, 0.67, 0.2-0.25 overlap child/man 0.5, woman/man 0.77, sheep/man 0.44. On the M288-companion tablets (P008105, P008490, P009185), the companion amount 60 reads as woman or child at 360 days, and larger ones as 'large animal' | C: a capacity record = one large ration plus one smaller ration (adult + dependant, or grain + a second commodity). Animal entries cannot be separated from children by amount (sheep and child needs overlap within 15%). Would support: the companion sign (M002~b, M297, M311~b) recurring in non-record lists with the same 1/3-2/3 ratio. Would kill: companions larger than mains on new record tablets |
| pe16:PE-16.3c | RANDOM RULERS on the PE records: 2,000 nonsense yardsticks vs real biology on half A (7 tablets), top 20 re-tested on half B | real biology ranks 1,125th of 2,000 (log evidence -69.3; random median -68.6, best -55.3); all 20 top rulers also beat biology on B | as on Ur III (cycle 1e: 217th of 600), fit quality does not single out biology. No information either way |
| pe16:PE-16.3d | VESSEL CHECK on the alias lattice (posterior of R x adult-man litres) | As fitted: N39C 4.6 l, N30D 9.3, N30C 18.6, N24 56, N39B 111, N01 557; P(any unit in 0.5-1.2 l) <= 0.02. Daily alias (u'/30): N39C 0.15 l, N30D 0.31, N30C 0.62 (P in bowl range 0.45), N24 1.85, N39B 3.7, N01 18.5. Yearly alias (12u'): nothing below 55 l | The fitted solution FAILS the bowl check: no PE unit would be bowl-sized. Only the /30 alias passes, with N30C ~ 0.6 l = one bowl and N24 ~ 1.9 l = one adult day. On that alias the record tablets are daily and ~12-day issues. POWER: because PE units are spaced only x2-6 apart, some unit falls in a x2.4-wide bowl window for 43% of random scales (N39C log-uniform 0.05-50 l). The all-entries estimate of cycle 2 (S) hits the bowl with a DIFFERENT unit (N39C 0.56 l, P 0.58; N30D 1.1 l, P 0.55). A bowl hit is cheap and does not pick one unit |
| pe16:PE-16.F1 | Positive control, Ur III (2,750 '-ta' lines, sila known) | blind litres per sila 0.83 [0.42-1.60] (truth ~1); daily wages and fodder vs monthly rations sorted right on 97-100% of tablets of known kind; shuffled amounts lose by 3,600 log units | PASS |
| pe16:PE-16.F2 | Calibration at PE size and by random rulers | 130- and 1,300-line Ur III subsets: nominal 90% intervals cover the truth 8/12 times; 4/12 alias by x10-12 (month <-> year). 600 random rulers on Ur III and 2,000 on PE fit as well as or better than real biology (ranks 217/600 and 1,125/2,000) | the method gives a scale but not a selection by fit; results live on a lattice x{1/30, 1, 12} |
| pe16:PE-16.F3 | PE two-line records (108 capacity lines, 14 tablets) | unit N39C = 3.2 adult-man-days [1.3-7.1] = 4.6 l [1.5-13.7] at the fitted periods (tablets split 30 / 360 days); random counts with the same numeral codes give 2.9-7.2 l, so the scale comes from which units are used; 4 other value sets 1.9-3.5 l | C |
| pe16:PE-16.F4 | All PE capacity entries (1,373; unknown head counts) | 0.56 l per N39C raw [0.28-1.09], ~1.4 l after the calibrated 2.5x bias of the head-count model; x10 across value sets; beats the digit null by 68-100 log units | C; disagrees with F3 by x3-8 |
| pe16:PE-16.F5 | Recipient classes in the two-line records | where both lines are capacity (P009000, P008403, P009123) the companion is 0.2-0.67 of the main line: an adult-sized ration plus a dependant-sized one (child, woman or small stock: these overlap in need). No class assignment is stable across posterior modes | C |
| pe16:PE-16.F6 | Vessel check (measured bevelled-rim bowls 0.5-1.2 l) | fitted point: no PE unit is bowl-sized (fails). /30 point: N30C 0.62 l (P 0.45) and N24 1.85 l = one adult day. | pe19:PE-19.1b | Blind seriation of all 1,585 tablets, 689 traits (base signs, variant forms, numeral codes, format bins), and a genre-light set (375: variants, numerals, format). Methods: CA axis 1, spectral (kNN cosine Fiedler), 2-opt TSP on Jaccard, unimodal latent-trait model (torch, restarts chosen by loss), consensus. Orientation fixed before scoring: late end = longer entries. All orders frozen with SHA-256 hashes (BVNF CA 2466f1d2cf284992, CONS d3bc5a552bdf3bd0; VNF CA 6c8b3bbf396b6821) | frozen before scoring | method ran blind |
| pe19:PE-19.1c | CONTROL planted drift: 40 (or 12) synthetic traits that are born / die along a hidden t (sigmoid, slope 8, base rate 3-20%) added to the real matrix; recover t | abs rho(order, t) 0.001-0.04 for every method, strong and weak plants, 2 seeds | FAIL: content (commodity / office) dominates; a plausible drift is invisible to every seriator |

| pe19:PE-19.1e | Scoring the frozen orders (pe19_score.py; 4,000 random orders; baselines: entry length only, line count only, number-system CA, site only; 10 trait-shuffled CA orders) | BVNF CA: Susa rho +0.47 (p 0.026), early sites AUC 0.76 (p 0.001), Malyan 0.92 (p 0.003); VNF CA similar; TSP reversed (rho -0.43); SPEC null. CA rho partialled on entry length 0.38, on line count 0.47 (n 17) | agreement is mostly tablet size / complexity + site (the orientation rule) and comes with failed calibrations. Grade C: 'bigger, longer-entry tablets are later' (would support: new stratified tablets follow it; would kill: a big tablet from 16-17) |
| pe19:PE-19.1 | Cycle verdict | controls fail, scores beat random but not the size baseline by a margin that n 17 can show | THE SCRIPT AGED is not detectable by generic seriation in this corpus; next: random trait subsets + partial CA |
