| pe20:PE-20.1 | FLOCK ARITHMETIC: 8 herd signs (M362, M367, M346, M006 and their ~a / @g partners) assigned blind to herd categories (F, M, YF, YM, Y, T, X; two species) and scored by a herd-biology likelihood (young 0.2-1.3 and males 0.02-0.3 per female, NB dispersion marginalised) on the 10 blocks of MDP 17,096+; 200,000 random draws + Gibbs. Controls: planted herds; Ur III Girsu/Umma herds with known categories; 200 all-entry and 200 within-sign shuffles | planted 5.45/8 coarse roles (chance 1.45); Ur III 1.8/7 exact (chance 0.8), udu-nita2 beats u8 as F; PE max 22.7 vs shuffles 13.1 / 14.7 (p 0.005); MAP M362 F, M362~a F, M006 M, M367 young, M346 X | coupling real; biological labels NOT calibrated (Ur III partial fail) |
| pe20:PE-20.2 | Held-out re-test on 35 records from 22 tablets; joint posterior of grouping; chain test vs herd lists 17,085/17,097; 300 random-ruler biologies | held-out +3.6 (within-sign p 0.008, all-entry p 0.002, beats 99.8% of random assignments); P 0.92 of an exact plain vs ~a split; lists track M362 (rho 0.75) not M362~a (-0.52); 35% of random rulers beat the pastoral box | PASS out of sample; biology does not select itself (as pe16) |
| pe20:PE-20.3 | Wether-aware model; null for the grouping (60 each: within-sign, all-entry, random column splits); leave-one-block-out imputation with planted and Ur III controls; predictions for broken cells of P008294 | wether model leaves Ur III wrong and spreads M367 over all classes; grouping p 0.033 (columns, all-entry), 0.08 (within-sign); imputation +0.86 bits/cell (nulls <= 0.09, p 0.048; planted 0.80); predictions rev 6.c M006 = 1 (0-4), obv 5.a M367 = 2 (0-6) | B: coupled herd counts and two reference counts (M362, M362~a); B-: blind plain / ~a split; C: M006 small male group; M367 = young demoted |
| pe20:PE-20.1a | PLANTED: 20 herd corpora with the 17,096+ missing-cell pattern, known roles (2 species: F, M, YF, YM / F, M; 2 X signs), annealed Gibbs MAP | coarse roles (F / M / young / X, species grouping) 5.45 of 8 correct (chance 1.45). F of the main species 20/20; young 40/40; M 15/20; second-species F 14/20; the two X signs are given a false biology role in 9/20 and 18/20 runs | the search works on true herd data at PE size; YF vs YM cannot be told apart (symmetric); unrelated small-count signs are often absorbed as M or young (false-positive risk for minor signs) |
| pe20:PE-20.1b | UR III positive control: 675 herd records from Girsu and Umma (CDLI; niga and dead lines dropped), categories known (u8, udu-nita2, sila4, kir11, ud5, masz2-nita2, masz2); full set and 20 PE-sized subsamples (10 records), each with 20 within-sign shuffles | full set: MAP udu-nita2 = F, u8 = young, sila4 = M, kir11 = F of a second species (1 of 7 exact). Truth scores -248 against the MAP's +665. Subsamples: 1.8 of 7 exact (chance 0.78), coarse 2.2 (chance 1.1); u8 = F in 12/20, udu-nita2 = F in 11/20, masz2 (kid) = M in 11/20; real beats its own shuffle max in 16/20 | PARTIAL FAIL. On real Ur III herding texts the blind search finds "large adult categories" and their coupling, but it does not separate ewes from rams/wethers or sheep from goats, and it reads kids as males. Biological labels from this method are not calibrated |
| pe20:PE-20.1c | PE: 200,000 random assignments; 4 Gibbs chains x 3,000 sweeps; annealed maximum. NULLS: 200 corpora with numbers shuffled across all entries and 200 with numbers shuffled within each sign (keeps each sign's magnitudes, breaks within-herd coupling), each maximised with the same effort | max LLR 22.7 (random draws: 99.9th pct 9.6). Nulls: all-shuffle max 13.1, within-sign max 14.7 of 200: p 0.005 for both (the minimum possible). MAP: M362 = F0, M006 = M0, M367 = young0, M346 = X; M362~a = F1, M367~a = young1, M346~a = M1, M006@g = young1 | the herd numbers carry within-herd coupling beyond magnitudes (pass). Biological labels: see 1b |
| pe20:PE-20.1d | Posterior support (species-collapsed marginals) | M362 F 0.97; M362~a F 1.00; M006 M 0.83; M367 young 0.82 (M 0.10); M367~a young 0.86 (M 0.12); M346 X 0.75; M346~a M 0.52 / young 0.41; M006@g M 0.30 / young 0.69 | two "breeding-female-like" counts (M362, M362~a); M367 and M367~a scale with them like young; M006 like a small male group; M346 fits nothing |
| pe20:PE-20.2a | HELD-OUT: cycle-1 MAP scored blind on the 35 records (no refit; rates marginalised over the prior). Nulls: the same assignment on 500 within-sign and 500 all-entry shuffles of the held-out numbers; 20,000 random assignments | held-out LLR +3.64; within-sign null mean -7.9 (p 0.008); all-entry null mean -14.8 (p 0.002); the MAP beats 99.8% of random assignments (random 99th pct 2.65) | PASS: the cycle-1 reading predicts herd numbers on other tablets better than chance and better than the same signs with shuffled numbers |
| pe20:PE-20.2b | Survivors: the 500 best random draws of cycle 1 on held-out data; rank transfer | top-500 mean held-out LLR -0.54 (64th pct of random); search-vs-held-out rank rho 0.03 among the top 500, 0.52 among 3,000 random assignments | only the optimum transfers. Fit transfers coarsely (good vs bad assignments), not among near-ties: the fine labels inside the top set are not supported |
| pe20:PE-20.2c | JOINT POSTERIOR (4 chains x 2,500 sweeps, 1,600 samples): which signs share a "species"? | P(exact split: {M362, M367, M346, M006} vs {M362~a, M367~a, M346~a, M006@g}) = 0.92. Each base sign is in a different group from its ~a partner: P 0.996 (M362), 0.99 (M367), 0.95 (M346), 0.97 (M006). P(M362 and M362~a both F, in different groups) = 0.82. M362 and M362~a get the same category 98% of the time when both are assigned, M367 and M367~a 34%, M006 and M006@g 31% | the search, which never sees the sign names, recovers the scribe's graphic split into a plain and a ~a series: two parallel herds or flocks per herd block, each with its own reference count. Null for this split in cycle 3 |
| pe20:PE-20.2d | CHAIN: herd size implied by an assignment (sum of non-X counts) in the 17,096+ blocks vs the first-column counts of the same herds in the herd lists 17,085 / 17,097 (pe5 link); MAP vs 20,000 random assignments; single signs | MAP rho 1.00 but on n 5 (blocks with every cell read); random assignments: median rho 0.89, MAP at the 73rd pct. Single signs: M362 rho 0.75 (n 11), M367 0.64 (9), M006 0.42, M346 0.09, M362~a -0.52 (11), M367~a -0.16, M346~a -0.10 | UNINFORMATIVE for the assignment (almost any sum of the big counts correlates). New detail: the lists track the PLAIN series (M362 and M367), not the ~a series. Whatever the herd lists count, it scales with the plain herd only |
| pe20:PE-20.2e | RANDOM RULERS: 300 nonsense prior boxes (young per female 0.05-8, males per female 0.005-3) vs the pastoral box, maximum score with equal effort; 6 Ur III PE-sized subsamples with 40 rulers each | PE: 35% of rulers beat the pastoral box (rank 105 of 301). 87% of the winners overlap the pastoral box (62% of all rulers do); the best are narrow low-rate boxes (young 0.1-0.65, males 0.05-0.3). Ur III: 10-80% of rulers beat real biology (mean 45%) | as in pe16, fit does not single out biology. It locates the rates: under the MAP, M367 = 0.22 per M362 (90%: 0.13-0.37), M367~a = 0.14 per M362~a (0.07-0.29), M006 = 0.07 per M362 (0.03-0.14), M346~a = 0.26 per M362~a. M367 is too few for a year's full young cohort (0.6-1.0) but fits one sex of surviving young, or a male class |
| pe20:PE-20.3a | WETHER-AWARE MODEL: adds W (non-breeding adults, mean omega*F, omega 0.05-3), to repair the Ur III failure of 1b. Ur III Girsu/Umma full set and 20 PE-sized subsamples; PE with 4 Gibbs chains and 100 within-sign nulls | Ur III: subsamples u8 = F 55%, udu-nita2 = F 35%, young right 53%, ud5 = F 15%. On the full set, truth with W scores +930 (sheep only) but udu-nita2-as-F scores +1,055: the data still prefer the wrong adult. PE: max 22.9, within-sign p 0.01. M362 F 0.84 (W 0.15); M362~a F 0.65 (W 0.32); M006 M 0.46 / W 0.31; M367 spreads over F, M, W, YF, YM (0.15-0.21 each) | the Ur III failure is not repaired: herd numbers cannot tell breeding females from other adults. In PE only "M362 and M362~a are the reference adult counts" survives the larger model. The young reading of M367 does NOT survive (posterior spread) |
| pe20:PE-20.3b | NULL FOR THE BLIND GROUPING (cycle 2c, P 0.92): 5 real runs vs 60 corpora each of within-sign shuffle, all-entry shuffle, and random 4/4 relabelling of the 8 columns | real: exact plain / ~a split in 5/5 runs (P 1.0). Within-sign shuffles: mean 0.10 (p 0.08); all-entry shuffles: 0.017 (p 0.033); random column splits: 0.028 (p 0.033) | the split is not a random grouping (p 0.03), but within-sign shuffles reproduce it 10% of the time. Part of it comes from each sign's magnitude, not only from within-herd coupling |
| pe20:PE-20.3c | LEAVE-ONE-RECORD-OUT IMPUTATION: for each 17,096+ block, refit the assignment on the other 9 blocks, then predict every assigned cell from the block's other cells (posterior predictive with rates from the other blocks) vs the generic law. Nulls: 20 within-sign shuffles. Controls: 8 planted herds; 8 Ur III subsamples (10 records) with a within-sign shuffle each | PE +0.86 bits per cell (57 cells); within-sign nulls -1.66 to +0.09 (p 0.048, the minimum for 20). Planted +0.35 to +1.11 (mean 0.80). Ur III median +0.29 (one subsample -16.9), its shuffles median -5.1; real beats shuffled in 8/8 | PASS: the PE numbers are predictable from their own herd block, as much as real (planted) herd data, and more than shuffles. This holds whatever the biological labels are |
| pe20:PE-20.3d | PREDICTIONS for broken cells of MDP 17,096+325+380 (P008294) under the cycle-1 MAP, checkable by collation of the tablet or photo | rev 6.c M006 (read "[...]"): 1 (80%: 0-4; generic 0-19). obv 5.a M367: 2 (0-6). obv 5.e M367~a: 3 (0-9); 5.f M346~a: 5 (1-10); 5.g M006@g: 3 (0-7). rev 2: M367~a 1 (0-3), M346~a 1 (0-3), M006@g 1 (0-2). Rev 2 header M362 (read "1(N14) [...]", so >= 10): model median 2 (0-12), which leans against the reading | open; would support: rev 6.c and obv 5 cells collate inside the 80% bands; would kill: two or more of them outside. The rev 2 header (>= 10) is already in the upper tail |

Ur III Girsu/Umma herds (known categories): 1.8/7 exact (chance 0.8); udu-nita2 (rams/wethers) beats u8 (ewes) as "breeding female" even with a wether category; kids read as males | PARTIAL FAIL: the method finds coupled counts, but its biological labels are not calibrated |
| pe20:PE-20.F2 | PE coupling | search max LLR 22.7 vs 200 all-shuffle and 200 within-sign nulls (p 0.005 each); held-out +3.6 (p 0.008 / 0.002; beats 99.8% of random assignments); leave-one-block-out imputation +0.86 bits per cell (nulls <= 0.09, p 0.048; planted 0.80) | B: within each herd block the numbers are tied together like a flock's, out of sample |

The herd lists 17,085/17,097 track the plain series only (M362 rho 0.75, M362~a -0.52) | B-: the scribe's ~a marking separates two parallel counted groups in each herd, which the numbers alone recover. | pe20:PE-20.F5 | Category labels | M006 = 0.07 per M362 (0.03-0.14): a small male group (C). | pe23:PE-23.1a | Ur III lines of KNOWN number type from CDLI bulk ATF (77,435 lines): LIVESTOCK (Drehem 'N udu/gu4/...', 49,536), PEOPLE ('N gurusz/geme2/erin2', 14,034), GRAIN (non-field 'X sze gur', 9,062), RATION (per-head '-ta' amounts, 3,644), ESTIMATE (field texts 'sze-bi X gur', computed harvest, 1,062), TARGET (field yield rate 'GAN2 X gur-ta', 97). Six fingerprints on the canonical notation (ROUND one-denomination share, LOW lowest denomination, FIVE v mod 5 = 0, LEAD1, REP same-tablet repeats, CONC value concentration) vs a smooth null (each value redrawn log-uniformly within x1.5, same denominations, 200x) | real minus null: ROUND counted +0.10-0.14, GRAIN +0.35, RATION +0.72, ESTIMATE +0.15, TARGET +0.43; REP counted 0.00-0.03, GRAIN 0.05, ESTIMATE 0.17, RATION 0.28, TARGET 0.66; FIVE counted +0.15, all capacity classes +0.73-0.80 | Every class heaps above a smooth null (scribes never write a smooth number). The textbook auditor rule fails: computed harvest ESTIMATES are LESS round (0.15 one-denomination) than measured grain (0.36); rule-made numbers (rations, yield rates) are the round, repeated ones |
| pe23:PE-23.1b | Separation: tablet-disjoint pseudo-groups of 60 entries (<= 10 per tablet), effect vectors (6 fingerprints minus 40-rep null), nearest centroid trained on tablet half A, tested on half B, 3 replicates; control = type labels shuffled across lines before grouping | accuracy 0.97 / 0.91 / 0.95 (chance 0.25); shuffled 0.38 / 0.38 / 0.29. Confusion rep 0: counted 30/30, measured 29/30, allocated 27/30, estimated 30/30; the three capacity types (measured, allocated, estimated) separate from each other, not just from counts | PASS: in Ur III, counted, measured, rule-allocated and computed-estimate numbers carry distinct digit fingerprints. | pe20:PE-20.check | (1) ATF status of the two cells. (2) CDLI photo. (3) Hand copy, enlarged 3x. The copy reads in columns; we aligned it with the ATF by matching the undamaged neighbouring counts, without looking at the predictions. (4) Search for a later collation | (1) Both cells are fully restored. Obv 5.a is "[M367] , [...]" and rev 6.c is "[M006?] , [...]", so no numeral is read and the sign is supplied by analogy (the rev sign is also queried). (2) The CDLI photo shows only edges and uninscribed fragment faces, not the two inscribed faces: no photographic check is possible. (3) The copy aligns cleanly with the ATF. On the obverse it reads, in order, obv 1.a-1.f; 2.b, 2.d (2 tens + 3), 2.e (7); 3 header (4 tens + 7), 3.a (8), 3.b (6), 3.d-g (10, 2, 1, 1); 4 header (40), 4.a (8-9), 4.d-g (6, 1, 3, 1); 5 header (1 ten), and in the next column 5.b (4-5 units visible along the edge), 5.c (2) and 5.d (damaged circle + balls). Obv 5.a falls between the 1 ten of the line-5 header (bottom of one column) and the top of the next column, which is the broken, hatched top-right corner: 0 impressions are drawn there because the surface is lost. On the reverse, line 6 reads header 2 tens + 1, 6.a 3, 6.b 1 ten + 9 (all match the ATF), and the next column has 6.d 6 tens (drawn over hatching), 6.e (damaged sign + about 2 units), 6.f 1 ten + 6 and 6.g 7. Rev 6.c and the 6.d sign fall in the break at the top of that column. 0 impressions are drawn; the bottom of the previous column below 6.b is blank surface. The copy cannot distinguish "M006 entry lost in the break" from "no M006 entry" (lines obv 1, 2 and rev 3, 4 also lack a c-entry). (4) No published reading of either cell was found: the CDLI ATF, which cites Dahl 2005, still has [...] | CANNOT BE CHECKED. Both cells fall in breaks of the original surface in the only available image, and no edition reads them. The predictions stay open (neither held nor failed). A check would need autopsy or new photographs of the Louvre pieces Sb 22286+22480+22534. Side result: the 1923 copy confirms every undamaged neighbouring count the model was fitted on, so the input data are sound |

| pe34:PE-34.3 | Factorisation (additive, scaling, rank-2 interaction; LOO with variant twins held out); M342 jackknife and unused singletons; modifier profiles | PE p 0.16 / 0.67 / 0.24; LINB 0.02 / 0.05 / 0.05; ARCH 0.28; drop M342 -> quantity p 0.28; |X+M342| less numbered than X on 5/7 bases (others 34%) | B negative: PE modifiers are not behavioural operators; C: M342 marks unnumbered / header-type forms |
| pe34:PE-34.1a | Additive transfer: for each compound whose modifier occurs on >= 1 other base (variants merged), predict behaviour = base vector + mean (compound - base) shift of the same modifier on other bases. Statistic dot = mean over targets of <observed residual, predicted residual> (residuals after base + generic compound shift); null: modifier labels shuffled among compounds (2,000x) | PE 69 targets: dot 0.013 vs null -0.005 +- 0.012, p 0.079 (cos 0.07). Raw squared error: base + modifier shift is WORSE than base alone (0.63 vs 0.43; single compounds are too noisy for unshrunk shifts); G vs shuffled p 0.24 | WEAK / NOT SIGNIFICANT over all 36 features |
| pe34:PE-34.1b | Same test on controls: proto-cuneiform admin (ARCH, 122 targets) and Linear B (LINB, 17 targets: :m / :f on OVIS, CAP, BOS, SUS, EQU; +SI, +PA, +O, +A ligatures) | ARCH dot -0.008 vs null -0.002, p 0.81. LINB dot 0.053 vs null -0.011 +- 0.026, p 0.012 (cos 0.12) | LINB positive control PASSES (sex markers and ligature syllables shift behaviour alike on different animals / goods). ARCH FAILS: proto-cuneiform inscribed signs (vessel x contents) are semantically compositional but their insets do not shift line behaviour alike across bases; behaviour follows the base |

Score: held-out error gain over base-only (mu + a_b); null: modifier labels shuffled (PE 150 / 20 / 20x, LINB same, ARCH 60x) | PE (69 held out): ADD gain -0.074 vs null -0.091 +- 0.016, p 0.16; SCALE p 0.67; CP p 0.24. LINB (17): ADD p 0.020, SCALE p 0.048, CP p 0.048. ARCH (122): ADD p 0.28. In every corpus the modifier term makes held-out error WORSE than base-only (noise); only the comparison with shuffled modifiers carries information | PE: NEGATIVE in all three model families; LINB positive control passes all three; ARCH fails. Multiplicative or interaction structure adds nothing in PE |

Other compounds: less numbered than their base in 34% (81/239) | C: adding M342 moves a sign off the numbered entry lines towards the tablet's opening line in 5 of 7 bases (vs 34% for other modifiers). Would support: new |X+M342| compounds on new tablets sit on unnumbered / first lines more often than X alone; would kill: they carry numbers as often as their bases |
| pe34:PE-34.3d | Modifier effect profiles (raw units; mean shift, z, share of bases agreeing), modifiers on >= 3 bases | M342: numbered -0.47 (5/5 bases agree), log count -0.59 (5/5), first line +0.24. 1(N14) inset: 'mod*' numerals +0.23 (z 3.2, but 2 of its 3 bases have 2 tokens). M388: entry-final +0.39 (3/4). @dup (|A+A|): first line +0.18, alone +0.29 (3-4 of 5). M288, M038, M136, M320, M365, 1(N24): no feature with |z| > 0.6 agreed by >= 80% of bases. Control LINB: :m and :f both raise the count (log count +0.91 / +0.65, 4/4 bases) relative to the bare animal sign; position effects only 3/4 | Meaning classes from behaviour alone: only M342 ('header / not-counted' marker, C) and weakly M388 ('entry-final', C; but M388 is itself a frequent framing sign (pe7), so this may be base-like behaviour of the modifier rather than an operator). No modifier marks a herd subtype, ration grade or container class detectably. Linear B :m/:f show the expected 'counted animal' shift, but the test cannot tell male from female (both shift the same way): behaviour profiles find the class 'sexed animal', not the sex |
| pe34:PE-34.3F | Cycle verdict | Factorisation (additive, scaling, interaction) finds no compositional behaviour in PE (p 0.16-0.67) while Linear B passes (p 0.02-0.05) and proto-cuneiform fails (p 0.28). The only transferable modifier is M342 (quantity family) | B (negative): most PE compound modifiers do not shift behaviour alike across bases; power: 20%-strength planted modifiers are found 5/5 at real sizes. C: M342 marks unnumbered / opening-line use. Calibration caveat: proto-cuneiform, a script with known semantic composition, also fails, so this says the modifiers are not behavioural operators, not that they lack meaning |
| pe34:PE-34.F1 | Controls | Planted modifiers at real compound sizes: 20% strength found 5/5, 0% 0/5. Shuffled-label calibration: 1/20, 0/20, 3/20 false positives (PE, ARCH, LINB). Linear B (:m/:f on 4-5 animals, ligature syllables): transfer p 0.012, tablet-disjoint p 0.001, factorisation p 0.02-0.05 (PASS). Proto-cuneiform (122 held-out |AxB| etc.): p 0.28-0.81 (FAILS: insets name contents but do not change line behaviour) | Method works where modifiers act on behaviour; it cannot see semantic composition that leaves behaviour unchanged |
| pe34:PE-34.F2 | Is PE compositional in behaviour? | All features: transfer p 0.08 (tablet-disjoint 0.19); additive / scaling / rank-2 factorisation p 0.16 / 0.67 / 0.24. A first run with variant forms counted as different bases gave p 0.006: an artefact, corrected | B (negative): PE modifiers are not behavioural operators (no shift >= the 20% planted effect), like proto-cuneiform insets and unlike Linear B sex markers |
| pe34:PE-34.F3 | Which modifier does transfer? | Quantity family p 0.006 (tablet-disjoint), carried entirely by M342 (drop it: p 0.28). |X+M342| is less often numbered than X on 5 of 7 bases incl. unused singletons (others: 34%); |M327+M342| is on the first line 87% | C: M342 turns a sign into an unnumbered, header-type form. Would support: new |X+M342| on new tablets unnumbered / first line more often than X; would kill: numbered as often as X |
| pe34:PE-34.F4 | Meaning classes from behaviour | M342 'header / not counted' (C); M388 'entry-final' (C, may be its own base behaviour); 1(N14) inset with 'mod*' numerals (C, 2 of 3 bases are 2-token forms). Linear B: :m and :f both read as 'counted animal'; the method cannot separate the sexes | Behaviour gives at most a coarse class; nothing is read |
| pe34:PE-34.F5 | Verdict | Not cracked. Compound signs are not sums of behaviour: bases carry behaviour (p 0.0005 in all three scripts), modifiers mostly do not | B negative; one C lead (M342) |

| x4:X-4.1a | Word-level arrow probes: 1,000 random probes per run (200 each: frequency classes, random classes, length, first sign, last sign; lags 1-4) on each document's word stream; survivor = abs z >= 3 on half A of the documents and z >= 2, same sign, on half B; 2 seeds. Exact null: each document read in a random direction (word order only). First version of this null reversed spellings too and leaked (Ur III entries 96 'survivors'); fixed and rerun | Null 0-0.5 per 1,000 everywhere. Survivors total / frequency-class, 8,000 words: Linear B 219 / 112, Ur III 153 / 36, Ur III entries 297 / 78, Linear B entries 186 / 52, syllabic Greek 113 / 65, Czech 142 / 58, Italian 22 / 7, Latin 12 / 10, German 3 / 2; PE entries 93 / 8.5; proto-cuneiform entries 0.5 / 0; Voynich 5.5 / 0.5. At 3,000 words: LB 105 / 43, UR3 68 / 11, GRC 39 / 22, Czech 60 / 28, Latin 0 / 0, German 0 / 0; LA 8.5 / 0, LA entries 35 / 14, PE 31 / 4, PC 0 / 0, Voynich 0 / 0 | List genre does NOT erase word arrows: known list languages carry the strongest frequency arrows of all (word order inside an entry: name, then logogram / person, then title). So the v23 Voynich absence is not a genre effect. But a real administrative system can have none: proto-cuneiform entries 0 of 2,000 (upper bound < 1.5 per 1,000), like the Voynich. Grade B |
| x4:X-4.1b | Layout control: first and last line of every document dropped (headers, totals) | LB 119 / 63 kept, UR3 88 / 5.5, Ur III entries 214 / 111, Czech 163 / 67; PE entries 93 -> 49 (freq 8.5 -> 11.5); LA 8.5 -> 0, LA entries 35 -> 0; LB entries 186 -> 0.5; Voynich 0.5 | PE keeps a body arrow (entries are ordered inside the tablet body; mostly length / first / last-sign classes, few frequency classes); LA's arrow is all headers and totals at this size. Grade B (PE), C (LA, low power: Latin and German also give 0 at 3,000 words) |

Presence of an arrow cannot prove language; absence matches a SLOT-type (independent words) process or a set of unordered entries. Grade B |
| x4:X-4.1d | Sign level: lag-1 sign-pair entropy production (excess over random-direction copies), KN-3 forward vs backward per document (sign-flip z), 300 random sign-partition probes | Excess irreversibility (bits) at 8,000: Voynich 4.27, LB 1.9-2.4, Ur III 1.8-2.6, prose 1.2-2.3, syllabic Greek 1.18; PE 0.32, PC 0.20; LA 0.55-0.61 (3,000). Sign trigram generators reproduce each corpus' value (TRI within +-0.4 bits of real everywhere except the CPV floor). KN direction weak everywhere (abs z <= 3.3; Voynich -0.1 to 0) | The Voynich glyph-level arrow is extreme against list languages too (Grade A, replicates v23). PE and PC have almost none: short sign groups from 1,600-2,100 sign types, no spelling. LA sits between (0.6). Sign arrows are spelling, reproduced by a trigram, so they do not separate language from generator (Grade A) |

Controls through the same pipeline: generators fitted to each corpus (TRI, SLOT, CPV, WBG), endings re-paired to stems at random (pair) | Alphabetic prose replicates v33: Latin 0.58-0.61, Italian 0.64-0.72, German 0.46-0.57, Czech 0.69-0.77 (64-94% of rules < 0.8). Fitted TRI / SLOT generators 1.00-1.11 everywhere | **The v33 gap test is a script-type test, not a language test.** A sign-trigram over syllables or logograms already fills the grid that a language fills; Linear B and syllabic Greek look 'gap-free' like the Voynich. The Voynich's ~1.0 stays far from alphabetic languages (its glyphs behave like letters on this axis, word length 4.5 units), but no conclusion about LA or PE can be drawn from g alone |

Needs the cycle-2b spread before grading |

Grade C for LA: at its size (3,000 words) the known languages spread 0.91-1.04 and LA (1.00-1.04) cannot be placed. But its LANG class does not recognise a single known language written in a syllabary or in list form; so the v31 'not language' axis is a prose-in-an-alphabet axis |

(REF0 -> REF2) | GEN 77% -> 49-57% (ZL), 72% -> 41-47% (X-4 samples); still the plurality class, with LANG 15-34% and GIBB 21-32% taking the rest | Grade B: the v31 generator verdict is partly a reference-set effect (it weakens by a third), but it does not flip to LANG; the Voynich does not look like a list language either (its votes differ from LB, UR3, PC, LA, PE, which are all near-unanimous) |

Floor: run a vs independent run b of the same generator | Floors 0.30-0.67 (median 0.51). Sign-level generators (TRI, SLOT, CPV) are caught in every corpus: AUC 0.93-1.00 for LA, LA entries, PE, PC, LB, UR3, Voynich, Latin, Italian, German, Czech; the one exception is syllabic Greek (TRI 0.76-0.79, SLOT 0.76-0.78). Word bigram (WBG, keeps the real lexicon) is the hard forger: LB 0.99-1.00, LA 0.93-0.96, PE 0.90-0.93, PC 0.90, UR3 0.91-0.95, UR3 entries 0.69-0.71, Voynich 0.77-0.82, prose 0.22-0.90 (German 0.22-0.26, Greek 0.53-0.59) | At this sample size every real corpus beats every sign-level forger, the Voynich included (A, replicates v21). The battery does not separate language from generator: the Voynich vs its word-bigram forger (0.77-0.82) sits inside the prose range, and LA and PE are caught exactly like Linear B, Ur III and proto-cuneiform. No LA or PE verdict from this axis (Grade B: uninformative, not negative) |

Controls: random-direction exact null (fixed after a leak), header / total removal, fitted generators, generator-vs-generator floors, label-permutation null (0 survivors), planted generators scored as test objects, leave-family-out votes | (1) Arrows: list languages have the STRONGEST word-frequency arrows (LB 112, Ur III 36-78 per 1,000 probes at 8,000 words); Voynich 0.5, proto-cuneiform 0, PE 8.5 (93 total, half survive header removal), LA 0 at 3,000 words where Latin and German also give 0. (2) Gaps: the raw v33 ratio is ~1 for EVERY syllabic / logographic corpus, known languages included; against its own generator Linear B 0.82, Ur III entries 0.88, syllabic Greek 0.90, LB entries 0.94, Ur III words 0.99; Voynich 1.04, proto-cuneiform 1.06, PE 1.08; LA 1.00-1.04 at a size where knowns spread 0.91-1.04. (3) Classifier: with list languages and list generators in the reference, Ur III (a known language) votes generator 71-95%, proto-cuneiform 98%, Linear B words 89-91% language; PE 93-99% and LA 56-91% generator, as do their own fitted generators; the Voynich's generator vote falls from 77% to 41-57% but stays the plurality. (4) Forgery: everything beats sign-level forgers (0.93-1.00); nothing separates language from generator | **Neither script looks Voynich-like in the way that matters, and neither can be certified language-like.** PE sits with proto-cuneiform on every axis (weak spelling arrow, G > 1, generator vote), not with Linear B or Ur III. LA is under-powered on every axis. The Voynich's profile is only partly a genre effect: its missing word arrow is not a list effect (lists have big arrows) but is shared by proto-cuneiform; its gap-free grid is shared by every syllabary; its generator vote weakens by a third with list references. Not cracked; nothing read |

| pe38:PE-38.1 | Cycle verdict | The pre-registered type system fails both controls (PC strict 0.00-0.04 vs shuffled 0.05; Ur III ~0), and on PE it forces only the default role, which shuffled corpora force just as often. Planted TOT/HDR are found because the rules name their positions. No PE sign is forced into a transaction-verb or total role; for TOT this is explained by the data (no total sign exists). Next: learn the rules from the controls by random guessing (cycle 2). | Negative (method fails control). |

| pe38:PE-38.2b | Held-out: top 20 true-label systems, the 20 permutation winners and 20 median-ranked systems re-run on PC-B and Ur III (3 chains x 100k). | PC-B: top 0.153 (0-0.26), permutation winners 0.110, median systems 0.136. | pe38:PE-38.3 | Cycle verdict | Forced roles do not replicate on held-out PE tablets beyond an entry-shuffled null, and in the control the replicating roles are wrong. No sign is forced into a transaction-verb or total role in any half. | Negative (calibrated). |
| pe38:PE-38.F1 | Controls | Planted total and header signs are found (TOT 2/2, HDR 1/2), planted verbs and list entries are not (0/2). Proto-cuneiform at PE size: strict accuracy of forced labelled roles 0.00-0.04 (entry-shuffled PC 0.05); Ur III 0-3 words forced, all wrong; szunigin never typed as total. 1,200 random rule systems fitted on proto-cuneiform: all 20 label permutations fit better than the true labels; held-out PC 0.15 and Ur III 0.12 macro recall vs 0.11-0.14 for null and median systems. Supervised role profiles transfer within proto-cuneiform (0.45 vs null 0.11) but not across scripts (PC -> Ur III 0.19 vs 0.15; Ur III -> PC 0.13 vs 0.15), and the accounting type-checker halves the within-script score (0.45 -> 0.22) | A (method limit): structure plus accounting rules cannot type signs at PE size; the method fails both controls |

Held-out agreement 0.243 vs null 0.246; proto-cuneiform halves agree at 0.64 while being 0% correct | B (negative): no forced role in PE beyond nulls |
| pe38:PE-38.F3 | Transaction verb or total marker? | No sign is forced VRB or TOT in any run, half or learned system. Verbs: absence is not evidence (the method cannot find planted verbs) | A (data): PE totals have no dedicated total sign (upper bound < 3/188 total lines could carry one undetected). Verb role: untestable here |
| pe38:PE-38.F4 | The one stable non-default role | |M327+M342| typed header/office in both halves of 3 of 4 splits; but also in 4 of 8 entry-shuffled halves (header lines kept). | pe38:PE-38.F5 | Verdict | Not cracked. Nothing is read. Type inference over sign roles fails its calibrated controls (as LA-40 did for Linear A), so no PE role assignment from it can be graded above C; the only firm output is a data fact about totals | Negative, calibrated |

| pe42:PE-42.1a | Calibration: one family planted into 30 closing tablets x 5 reps per corpus; recovery = planted family among the explainers; enrichment = planted-family count vs the noise null on the planted set. | Recovery 1.00 for OMIT, DOUBLE, CONV, CARRY, XCARRY, NOCARRY, DIGIT in all three corpora; SYS 0.80 PE / 0.70 PC / 0.33 MES; FOREIGN 0.72-0.90. Enrichment vs noise: OMIT 164 vs 44, DOUBLE 150 vs 23, CONV 116 vs 25, SYS 86 vs 40 (PE); NOCARRY 135 vs 61 (PC); CARRY 150 vs 118 and DIGIT 150 vs 135 (MES), FOREIGN 97 vs 96. | Control passes for tablet-specific families (OMIT, DOUBLE, CONV, SYS, NOCARRY). Matches what an additive, single-system bookkeeper would make. No labelled 'known error' set exists in the CDLI dump (213 editor error notes, almost all lexical), so this is a sanity check, not a recovery of labels. |

OMITO 13 (5.0, p 0.005), DIGIT 16 (4.6, p 0.005), FOREIGN 82 (70.0, p 0.005); CONV 4 (7.9, p 0.97), SYS/WHOLE 0, CARRY 2 (p 0.72). | Same profile as PC: omission + digit slip. CONV is below the null in both controls, so a CONV excess is not a generic artefact of the method. |
| pe42:PE-42.1d | PE, same. | 31/60 explained (totals 21.2, p 0.005; noise 31.7, p 0.66). OMITO 5 (noise 1.5 p 0.025; totals 2.5 p 0.095; shuffle p 0.005). WHOLE 5 (totals 0.8 p 0.005; noise 3.5 p 0.28). CONV 13 (totals 6.0 p 0.015; noise 8.8 p 0.08). DOUBLE 4 (n.s.), DIGIT 3 (totals 1.4 p 0.13; fewer than the controls), CARRY / XCARRY / NOCARRY 0, OMITH (eye-skip) 0. | PE's error profile differs from both controls: no digit or carry slips beyond chance, few omissions, and a CONV/WHOLE excess that neither control shows. |
| pe42:PE-42.1e | What the WHOLE tablets are. | All 5 (P008229, P008241, P008784, P009107, P009296) are written only in N01/N14, were typed 'count' (ambiguous numerals), and close EXACTLY when N14 = 6 N01 (e.g. P009107 12+13+13+13 -> 8+9+9+9 = 35 = 5(N14) 5(N01); P008241 thirteen 1(N01) entries -> 1(N14) 7(N01) = 13). Every one of their decimal discrepancies is +4 per N14 of the total. Total-line signs: M288 (2), M297, |M217+M388|, M122. | Not scribal errors: OUR error. The 'wrong system' process recovers that these tablets are in the capacity (N14 = 6) system although written with count-looking numerals. Resolves two earlier open predictions: P009107 (pe 'total should read 51', read 55: 55 is right under N14 = 6) and P008784 ('line 2 should read 8(N01)': not needed). |
| pe42:PE-42.1f | What the CONV tablets are. | 13 tablets; 4 are the WHOLE tablets again. Of the other 9, 5 are capacity tablets with factors 3, 3, 5, 1/6, 10 on entries carrying N39B/N24/N30C; count tablets: P008955 (M288; 1(N45) 1(N14) = 70 if N45 = 60, not 110), P008867 (x3), P008004 (1/10), P008316 (1/2). | Most CONV hits look like value-map slips (one code read with another system's value) rather than commodity conversions. Cycle 2 adds code-level system slips and asks which survive a family-wise null. |
| pe42:PE-42.1 | Cycle verdict | Controls pass (planted families recovered; PC and MES give an omission + digit-slip profile and no CONV/SYS excess). PE failing totals are better explained than reassigned totals (31 vs 21), but not by the PC/MES error types: no carry or digit excess, no eye-skip omissions; the excess is in WHOLE (5 tablets, all closing under N14 = 6) and CONV. | Positive (B, data): 5 'failing' count tablets are capacity tablets written in N01/N14. Error-type profile: grade C. |
| pe42:PE-42.2a | Hypothesis space. | PE: 1,157 hypotheses explain >= 1 tablet in real or null runs (355 in real); PC 777 (152); MES 2,150 (160); planted PE 1,584-1,612. | Search size as intended (thousands); most hypotheses are singletons. |
| pe42:PE-42.2b | Per-hypothesis p is useless for singletons (a sign-specific hypothesis that explains 1 real tablet is almost never matched by noise: p 0.003 for ~100 keys in every corpus). Family-wise test on the MAX sign-conditioned count. | PE: max 4 (WHOLE cap on tablets with M388) vs null max 2.20, p 0.047. Planted M057: 4 (SYS cap on S:M057, the planted rule, ranked first) p 0.059. Planted M388: p 0.050. PC: max 2 vs 2.23, p 0.97. MES: 3 vs 5.25, p 0.997. | Controls give no false sign-linked error (PC, MES). PE's top link sits at the edge of the family-wise threshold, at the same level as a planted 4-tablet link. |

| pe39:PE-39.1a | Setup. Sentences = lines with >= 1 sign and >= 1 numeral: PE 6,531 lines (pe_corpus.json), proto-cuneiform 16,079 lines (pe2_pc_corpus.json, Uruk IV/III admin). Shared tokens = numeral code x count ('N14:2'); every sign is language-private. Model: shared embedding, shared bidirectional GRU encoder, attention GRU decoder with a target-language tag, output masked to the target script plus numerals; 2,000 steps (500 denoising only, then denoising + online greedy back-translation both ways). Held-out 15% of tablets per side. Lexicon: for each source sign, the target sign maximising P(t|s) - P(t) over the translated corpus. Held-out gain of a pair = rise in held-out round-trip NLL when t is banned from the decoder, minus the rise for 3 frequency-matched random bans. Web check: no PE <-> proto-cuneiform UNMT found (X-2 did embedding alignment, not generative back-translation) | built; 4 runs of ~35-40 min each on a shared, overloaded machine (load 25 on 4 cores) | method ready |
| pe39:PE-39.1b | KNOWN-ANSWER CONTROL: proto-cuneiform split by tablet; half 1 renamed ('pq|SIGN'), cut to PE size; half 2 the target. Truth = identity. Same model, seed 0 | identity recovered for 4/175 source signs with >= 15 occurrences, 2/85 with >= 40 (2.4%; chance ~0.7%). Translations are SHARP and WRONG: mean P(t|s) 0.69 (SZE -> N57 0.95, BA -> U4 0.96, NUN -> GAL, GI -> ME, SI -> BU, UD5 -> SZITA 0.99); only EN -> EN right. No-learning baseline (nearest numeral-profile, Hellinger) on the same split: 17/85 (20%), 3 seeds 15-17/83-85 | **FAILS the control.** Back-translation finds a confident private substitution code, a permutation mostly among frequency neighbours, not the true one; it does WORSE than simply matching numeral profiles (2% vs 20%) |
| pe39:PE-39.1c | Numbers-shuffled control on the known-answer pair (numeral groups permuted across lines of the renamed half) | identity 1/175 (>= 15), 1/85 (>= 40); profile baseline 2-4/83 | with shuffled numbers the profile baseline collapses (20% -> 3%), so numbers do carry the identity; the neural model is at the shuffled level with or without them |
| pe39:PE-39.1d | Held-out back-translation as a selection criterion (the prompt's 'improve held-out back-translation'), known-answer pair | mean pair gain +0.60 nats (24/30 pairs > +0.1) for pairs that are 29/30 wrong; the one correct pair (EN) +0.18; numbers-shuffled +0.55 | **Held-out round-trip gain does not mark correct translations.** Any consistent code earns it; the criterion is blind to truth |
| pe39:PE-39.1e | Real run PE <-> PC and the PE numbers-shuffled control (seed 0) | real: M288 -> N57, M218 -> SANGA, M388 -> UDU, M387 -> SAL, M297 -> BA, M346 -> DUG, M124/M009/M354 -> EN, M036 -> GAR; held-out round-trip NLL 1.00, numerals copied unchanged in 78% of lines (control pair 97%). Shuffled PE numbers: round-trip NLL 0.89 (better than real), same top translation for 34/154 signs, pair gain +0.57 (real +0.41) | real and number-shuffled PE are indistinguishable by every model score; given 1b, the real lexicon is uninterpretable (M388 -> UDU, M036 -> GAR look like readings but the control shows such pairs arise without truth). Nothing graded above C |
| pe39:PE-39.1f | Ur III <-> proto-cuneiform second known-answer pair (abs numeral bridge: system + quantile bin; gold of 37 Ur III words -> PC signs frozen before the runs in data/pe39_ckpt/gold_ur3_pc.json); neural run not reached (compute), profile baseline only | profile baseline 0/26-0/37 correct in 3 seeds (and 0 shuffled) | with only system + magnitude as the bridge, numbers carry no identity across two cuneiform corpora 1,000 years apart; this pair is dead before any learning |
| pe39:PE-39.1 | Cycle verdict | UNMT with numerals as the only bridge fails its known-answer control (2% vs profile baseline 20%), its stability/held-out criterion is blind to truth, and real PE is not distinguishable from number-shuffled PE | **KILLED in the plain form.** Next: force the numbers into the model (embeddings built from numeral profiles) so the network cannot invent a private code |
| pe39:PE-39.2a | Arch 'prof' (tools/pe39_common.py GRUProf): every token's embedding = W . sqrt(numeral-profile) + a small free part (x0.1 init); output layer tied to the same embeddings, so a PE sign and a PC sign start close only if they sit with the same numerals. Same denoising + back-translation, 1,000 steps, emb 48 / hid 96. Known-answer control (renamed proto-cuneiform half, truth = identity), 2 seeds | identity 10/175 and 11/173 (>= 15 occurrences), 8/85 and 8/83 (>= 40): 6-10%, up from 2% for the plain GRU (cycle 1); profile baseline on the same splits 18-21/175, 13-14/85 (11-16%) | better than cycle 1 but STILL BELOW the no-learning numeral-profile match. Back-translation scrambles part of what the numbers give (e.g. seed 0 swaps SZE <-> BA, which the profile had right) |
| pe39:PE-39.2b | Numbers-shuffled known-answer control (renamed half's numerals permuted) | nn 4/175, 4/173 (2%); profile 3-4/175 | numbers carry the 6-10%; with them shuffled both fall to 2% |
| pe39:PE-39.2c | Seed stability in the control: share of frequent source signs (>= 15) with the same top translation in both seeds; precision of the stable ones | seeds agree on 5/175 signs (3%); stable pairs correct 3/5 (GAR -> GAR, HI@g -> HI@g, GAN2 -> GAN2; wrong SUHUR -> MASZ, SIG7 -> NAMESZDA); stable + held-out gain > 0.1: 1/1 (GAR, +0.68). Numbers-shuffled: 3 stable, 1/3 correct (SIG2) | seed stability is rare but, when it occurs, precision is 60% (3/5) against 33% (1/3) shuffled. Too few to calibrate; cycle 3 adds seeds |
| pe39:PE-39.2d | Real PE <-> PC and numbers-shuffled PE, 2 seeds each | seeds agree on 3/154 PE signs (2%): M036 -> HI@g (118 occ., gain +0.83), |M036+1(N30D)| -> SUHUR (87, +1.21), M386 -> MUD (55); shuffled PE: 2/154 stable (M264 -> BAR, M109 -> AN). Held-out round-trip NLL real 0.34 vs shuffled 0.39 (better with real numbers, reverse of cycle 1); mean pair gain 0.40 vs 0.40. No-learning profile for real PE is a hub: M388, M387, M376, M124 -> TUG2 | real PE yields as many stable translations as number-shuffled PE (3 vs 2): **the PE <-> PC translation is at the noise floor.** M036 -> HI@g is the one real pair to watch (M036 is a PE capacity-class sign; the control pair GAR -> GAR shows capacity pairs can be right) - grade C |
| pe39:PE-39.2 | Cycle verdict | numeral-built embeddings lift the known-answer recovery from 2% to 6-10%, still under a plain profile match (11-16%); real PE gives no more stable translations than shuffled PE | **No translation survives.** Back-translation adds nothing over counting which numerals a sign sits with; its own stability is rare (2-3% of signs). Next: many cheap seeds to calibrate 'stable across seeds' as a filter |
| pe39:PE-39.3a | Numeral-embedded model ('prof'), 600 steps, 4 more seeds of the known-answer control (6 with cycle 2); 2 transformer seeds (2 x 2 layers, d 96, plain embeddings); 2 more numbers-shuffled control seeds | prof identity per seed 6-9/174 (3-5%), profile baseline on the same splits 17-20/174 (10-11%); transformer 2/174 and 2/175 (1%); numbers-shuffled 3-6/175 (nn) and 1-4 (profile). Across settings, better held-out round trip goes with more correct pairs (GRU 1.15 -> 2%, transformer 0.73-0.82 -> 1%, prof 600 steps 0.55-0.60 -> 3-5%, prof 1,000 steps 0.43 -> 6%) but never reaches the profile baseline | A (method limit): no architecture tried beats counting numerals; back-translation subtracts from the numeral signal |
| pe39:PE-39.3b | Calibrate seed agreement on the control (modal target in >= k of 6 seeds) | k >= 2: 60 stable, 10 correct (17%); k >= 3: 9, 5 correct (56%); k >= 4: 3/3 (SZE, HI@g, GU7); k >= 5: 2/2; k = 6: none. Stable + held-out gain > 0.1: 1/1 at k >= 4, 2/4 at k >= 3 | B: seed agreement is a real precision filter (56-100% at k >= 3-4) but recovers only 3-5% of the vocabulary even when the answer is in the data |
| pe39:PE-39.3c | Nulls for agreement. (i) Hub-preserving null (each seed's lexicon permuted among sources +-8 frequency ranks, 300x): passes the control (p 0.003) but ALSO passes per-seed-shuffled PE (k >= 3 p 0.01) - too weak, dropped. (ii) FAIR null: two fixed numeral shuffles of PE (REALSHUFX0, X1), each trained with 6 seeds on identical data, like real PE. (Cycle 2's per-seed shuffles had different data per seed and were unfair) | stable PE signs, real vs X0 vs X1: 4 seeds (all 600 steps) k >= 2: 41 vs 21 / 30, k >= 3: 6 vs 3 / 0; 6 seeds k >= 3: 18 vs 8 / 7, k >= 4: 4 vs 2 / 0, k >= 5: 0 / 0 / 0 | real numbers give about twice the stable translations of shuffled numbers: about half of the real stable pairs are expected under the null. Weak signal, not a lexicon |
| pe39:PE-39.3d | What the real stable pairs (k >= 3/6) are | k = 4: M388 -> EN (406 occ.; gain +0.07; ALSO stable 3/6 in null X1, so frequency hub), M136 -> SZE (64), M111 -> SZE (46), M147 -> U4 (20); k = 3: M124 / M295 / M318 -> AN, M056 -> SZE, M059 / M075 -> BA, M354 -> GAR (gain +0.67), M367 -> HASZHUR, M102 -> U8, M101 -> NUN, M099 -> GA2, M112 / M379 -> HI@g, M228 -> ZATU694. Hub targets: SZE (3 PE signs), AN (3), BA (2), HI@g (2); nulls also send 3-4 signs each to SZE, EN, AN. Numeral profiles: M136 and M111 are at the PE average (N01 0.48-0.52, N14 0.20-0.22, N39B 0.11-0.20); M354 and PC GAR share the N51 (bisexagesimal) notation (8% vs 13%) | the one notation-explained pair, M354 -> GAR, restates the known B-system class (grade C, class level). M136 / M111 -> SZE and M147 -> U4 are not explained by notation and not in either null: grade C- |
| pe39:PE-39.3e | SECOND KNOWN-ANSWER PAIR: Ur III (cut to PE size) <-> proto-cuneiform, bridge = system (count / capacity) + quantile bin; gold (37 words) frozen before the runs; prof model, 4 seeds | gold-correct per seed 1/36, 1/37, 3/36, 1/37 (u8, tug2, sze / udu / geme2, u2), different in every seed; stable >= 3/4: 6 pairs, all to hub targets (sila3 / kasz / zi3 -> TAR; la2, 1(disz)-kam, 2(disz)-kam -> BA), 0 of 2 gold-scorable correct (kasz, u4 wrong); at 2/4: gur -> SZE, sze -> GAR (class level) | **FAILS.** With a coarse number bridge across 1,000 years, stability marks hubs, not translations. Only class level shows (capacity unit -> grain) |
| pe39:PE-39.3 | Cycle verdict | stability is a valid filter only where the bridge is strong (same script, shared codes): there k >= 4 is 3/3 correct. For real PE it yields 4 pairs at k >= 4 against 0-2 under fair nulls, and one of the four (M388 -> EN) is a frequency hub that also appears in a null | **No sign-to-sign translation is established.** Leads graded C / C- only |
| pe39:PE-39.final | Three cycles (tools/pe39_common.py, pe39_run.py, pe39_profile.py, pe39_sum.py, pe39_calib.py, pe39_calib2.py, pe39_hubnull.py; 44 trained translation systems; checkpoints in data/pe39_ckpt/, git-ignored). PE lines (6,531) and proto-cuneiform admin lines (16,079) as two languages; only numeral tokens shared (code x count, 'N14:2'). Shared-encoder seq2seq with denoising autoencoding and online iterative back-translation; architectures: plain GRU (cycle 1), GRU whose embeddings are built from each sign's numeral profile (cycles 2-3), small transformer. Lexicon = target sign most raised by the source sign in translation; held-out gain = round-trip NLL rise when the target is banned, vs frequency-matched random bans. Controls: proto-cuneiform half renamed (truth = identity), Ur III <-> proto-cuneiform (37-word gold frozen first), numbers shuffled on both control and PE (per seed and two FIXED shuffles x 6 seeds), no-learning numeral-profile match, hub-preserving null | Known-answer pair: plain GRU 2% of frequent signs right, numeral-embedded 3-10%, transformer 1%, against 10-16% for simply matching numeral profiles; translations are sharp (P(t|s) ~0.7-0.99) and mostly wrong. Held-out back-translation gain is as large for wrong pairs as for right ones (+0.60 vs +0.18 nats). Seed agreement does filter: >= 4 of 6 seeds agree on 3 signs, all 3 correct (>= 3: 5/9). Ur III pair: stable pairs are all hubs, 0 of 2 gold-scorable correct. Real PE: 4 pairs at >= 4/6 (M388 -> EN, M136 -> SZE, M111 -> SZE, M147 -> U4) vs 2 and 0 under the two fixed numeral shuffles; 18 vs 8 / 7 at >= 3/6; M388 -> EN also appears in a null | **Not cracked; nothing read.** Unsupervised translation with numbers as the only shared vocabulary does worse than counting numerals, even when both sides are the same script. Real numbers produce about twice as many seed-stable PE -> PC translations as shuffled numbers, so a weak number-carried signal exists, but at most a handful of pairs and about half are expected by chance |

| pe45:PE-45.1 | THE NAMES ARE A POPULATION THAT BREEDS. 85,000 random naming grammars (free Zipf vocabulary, god-like constants, lineage markers, 3-generation genealogies with father-to-child element copying, kin-drawn tablets, tablet-local pools) simulated as whole populations on the PE and Ur III templates; rejection ABC on 13 statistics, 12 more held out (shared-sign network, cliques, co-travel, slot fixity). Controls: 40 planted grammars; Ur III opaque names; nulls: names resampled across tablets, signs shuffled within names | Planted: vocabulary, lineage count, pool, marker prob recovered (r 0.45-0.95); god-like signs and inheritance NOT (r -0.07 to 0.28). PE passes held-out (p 0.24); Ur III fails it (p 0.025: fixed initial heads). Model choice (calibration AUC POOL 0.90, KIN 0.85, INH 0.63): PE pool-only 0.40, pool+kin 0.39, kin-only 0.14, neither 0.07 (null neither 0.62) | B: tablet clustering (as pe7). C: tablet pool over kinship (BF 1.6). Inheritance not identifiable |
| pe45:PE-45.2 | Per-sign classes read off 640 survivor populations (gradient-boosted classifier on 12 sign features). Controls: planted (AUC god 0.99, marker 0.84); Ur III real god signs; within-name and across-tablet nulls | Ur III: real gods AUC 0.55; the class picks ur-, lugal-, lu2-, -la, -a (name frame). PE 'god-like' = M009, M054, M001, M056, M010 (final), M157, M210 (initial); 33 tablet-clumped 'markers' led by M370, M305, M317, M373 (with herd/ration signs M376, M362, M006) | god reading FAILS its control; reinterpreted as name-frame elements (C). Marker class mixes kin-like and topic signs |
| pe45:PE-45.3 | Out of sample: real Ur III families (244 father + sons) vs random tablets; split-half replication; prediction 'families travel together' | Families invisible (AUC 0.52-0.54, sharing 1.17x); split-half rho no better than null (0.57 vs 0.52-0.60); markers do not bring partners to held-out tablets (-0.06 +- 0.11). 59/178 PE tablets clustered at p <= 0.01 (P008104, P008323, P008788, P008291) | kin-group tablets not supported (C-, demoted); the method cannot see kinship where names do not inherit elements |
| pe45:PE-45.final | `loops/pe45_final.txt` | No breeding population visible; frame elements M056/M010/M001 (final) and M157/M210 (initial) are the only new grade C classes | negative, calibrated blind spot recorded |
| pe45:PE-45.1a | Grammar space (17 parameters): Zipf free vocabulary; 0-15 god-like constants (prob, slot, skew); 5-1,000 lineages sharing marker signs (prob, slot, sharing); 3 generations with fan-out 1-5 where a child copies one element of the father's name (prob, same or shifted slot); tablets drawn from 1+Pois kin lineages (share kin_frac) or from the whole population; optional tablet-local sign pool. Bank: 60,000 random grammars on the PE template, 25,000 on the UR3 template (85,000 simulated populations). 25 statistics: block A (frequency, initial/final concentration, repetition, name recurrence, same- vs cross-tablet sharing) for fitting; block B (per-sign slot fixity, positional sharing, tablet cliques, co-travel of rare signs, dispersion, shared-sign network degree/transitivity/giant component, top-5 slot profile) HELD OUT | bank built | - |
| pe45:PE-45.1b | Planted control: 40 grammars drawn from the prior, simulated on the PE template, fitted with the same bank (top 200 of 60,000 on block A) | recovered (r truth vs posterior median): vocabulary size 0.89, Zipf 0.95, number of lineages 0.64, pool 0.68, fan-out 0.58, marker prob 0.53, kin share 0.45. NOT recovered: number/prob/slot of god-like signs (r -0.02 to 0.28), father-to-child inheritance prob (r -0.07) and slot shift (0.01). 90% intervals cover 0.82-1.00. Held-out block B never rejects a planted truth (0/40 at p<0.05) | method sees lineage/pool structure; it CANNOT see god-like constants or genealogical inheritance at this corpus size (calibrated blind spot) |
| pe45:PE-45.1c | PE fit; held-out check of block B against the 200 survivors | posterior: vocabulary e^5.9 = ~370 free signs, Zipf 0.90, ~185 lineages [20-900], marker prob 0.47, kin share 0.51 [0.12-0.94], pool 0.35 [0-0.76]; god and inheritance parameters = prior. Held-out: PE lies inside the survivors' spread (p 0.235; all 12 held-out statistics between the 12th and 85th percentile) | the grammar family is adequate for PE: survivors predict the network, clique, co-travel and slot statistics they were not fitted on |
| pe45:PE-45.1d | Nulls: names resampled across tablets (5); signs shuffled within names (5) | across-tablet null: pool median 0.00, kin share 0.23-0.31, marker prob 0.26-0.31 (all fall). Within-name null: posterior identical to PE (the fitting statistics barely use order) | tablet structure in PE is real (as pe7); slot claims must come from block B and the classifier, not from block A |
| pe45:PE-45.1e | UR3 control fit (own bank) | posterior: no pool (0.00 [0-0.19]); held-out block B REJECTS the survivors (p 0.025): Ur III shares elements at the same position far more than any grammar (pos_same and pos_init at the 98-99th percentile, top-5 initial share at 100th) and has no tablet cliques (8th percentile) | Ur III names (fixed initial heads ur-, lugal-, lu2- plus a god) are OUTSIDE this grammar family; PE is not. The model would flag a Sumerian-type naming system as misfit |
| pe45:PE-45.1f | Model choice inside the bank (k=300 nearest on all 25 statistics). Mechanisms: POOL (p_pool>0), KIN (marker prob x kin share > 0.25), INH (inheritance > 0.5). Calibration: 400 leave-one-out bank sims | calibration AUC: POOL 0.90, KIN 0.85, INH 0.63. PE: P(POOL) 0.79 (prior 0.50, across-tablet null 0.35-0.44), P(KIN) 0.53 (prior 0.37, null 0.03-0.07), P(INH) 0.56 (prior 0.44, null 0.46-0.49). Joint: pool-only 0.40, pool+kin 0.39, kin-only 0.14, neither 0.07 (null: neither 0.61-0.64). UR3: P(KIN) 0.38 = its null 0.39-0.40; P(INH) 0.25 (below prior) | tablet structure in PE is explained by a tablet pool, or pool plus kin markers; KIN alone is the least favoured (Bayes factor pool-only : kin-only = 1.6, weak). INH is not identifiable (AUC 0.63) and PE does not differ from its null. Ur III: no kin, little inheritance (matches the known 1.1x father-son sharing) |
| pe45:PE-45.2a | 300 corpora simulated from the 200 PE survivors (true class of every sign known) train a gradient-boosted classifier on 12 corpus-internal sign features (frequency, rank, tablet dispersion, initial/final/middle share, slot entropy, partner diversity, tablet clumping, rare co-signs on its tablets, share in recurring names, name length). Planted control: 40 further survivor simulations | planted AUC: god-like 0.99 (11 per corpus), marker 0.84 (43), kin tablets 0.72 | classes are recoverable when the grammar is true |
| pe45:PE-45.2b | Ur III control (classifier trained on the UR3 survivors; truth = 17 god signs with freq >= 3, determinative deleted) | planted AUC inside UR3 sims 0.95, but on REAL Ur III gods AUC 0.55 (frequency-matched 0.56; frequency alone 0.51). Real gods (nin, szul, lamma, nansze, en) score 0.03-0.11 and behave like free elements: many gods share one variable slot | CONTROL FAILS for gods. What the 'god-like' class finds in real names is the NAME FRAME (constant head or suffix, e.g. ur-X, lugal-X, X-la), not the deity |

PE's ~3,000-4,500 named agents (C) exceed residents at one time (630-1,960) 2-5x but sit inside the adults present over the PE span (1,800-7,700): coherent with ONE town recorded over several generations, without a regional catchment. Linear B: logogram/term 56% (base 28%), names 28% (base 41%); GRA, SA, TA, o-na-to at PY, VIN, FAR at TH | A (control fact) and the key INVERSION: at PE sample sizes the outpost-specific class is made of COMMODITIES AND GENRE WORDS (what that office handled), not of local names; names are too rare to show any site pattern. Non-planted passes: only CO echoes of the planted LR pairs and the base worlds' own artefacts (W1: HS M157; W2: ES M288->M288). True false passes 0 | The rule recovers planted ORDERED long-range and header constraints with no new false passes; header -> system planted at rate 0.8 is found 1/2. |

No commodity order, no ration-worker link | Known real-world constraints come back with the words opaque: totals and the end-of-tablet date formula. Ration-to-worker links and commodity orders do not appear: they are adjacent pairings or within-line facts a surface forger copies. |

Provisionally an ARTEFACT; cycle 3 tests it with a header-copying forger. |

| pe56:PE-56.2c | Positive controls | PL1: planted LR M146 -> M293 45 vs 3.5 (12.9x), M193 -> M110 19x, M069 -> M210 5.5x; PL2: M269 -> M325 5.2x, |M296+M296| -> |M131+M388| 10.6x, M069 -> M059 5.8x (planted residues 5-19x; world artefacts 1.5-2.2x). UR1: the date frame (LAST mu 4.8x, ba-hul 5.0x, us2-sa 5.9x, the king 5.8x; CO ki/mu 2.7x), totals (TOT 12 vs 0.9), EF szunigin chain 3.1x | Real constraints transfer at 3-19x; forger-ensemble artefacts at 1.5-2.7x. PE has nothing above 2.7x except one 2-instance CO pair. |
| pe56:PE-56.3a | Calibration on Ur III (UR2), proto-cuneiform (PC1, PC2) | Ur III: TOT udu = running sum -> ORDER+BIND (R 11 vs PERM 3.3, VPERM 3.1); szunigin -> szunigin chain -> ORDER (38 vs 17.9); the date frame (LAST mu, ba-hul, us2-sa, the king; CO ki/mu, iti/mu) -> TABLET. PC1: 4 ORDER residues (EF/ES P_MA -> P_HASZHUR 16 vs PERM 4.0; P_|U4x1(N57)| -> P_KISIM 8 vs 1.4; P_ME -> P_ZATU753 9 vs 1.7), LAST P_GU7 TABLET (37 vs HDR 4.2), runs of equal magnitudes (MM 1->1 ... 7->7) TABLET; PC2 0 ORDER at 1e-3 (EF P_UDU -> P_|U4x1(N57)| 10 vs PERM 3.0, p 1e-3) | The oracles sort known constraints correctly: arithmetic totals need both the order and the numbers; Ur III date formulas are whole-tablet; proto-cuneiform keeps a few fixed entry orders that survive permutation of the tablet's own lines. |
| pe56:PE-56.3b | Planted worlds PL1, PL2 (planted rate 0.8, partner on a random later entry) | Planted LR pairs: 1 of 8 testable are ORDER (M146 -> M293 26 vs PERM 11.3, p 1e-4); the rest TABLET, TOPIC or HEADER (PERM 2-10 vs R 4-20, p 0.004-0.06). Planted HE pairs: TABLET (R 4 vs HDR 0, TOPIC 0.07). Header -> system: HEADER (explained). | Permutation power is low for a partner placed anywhere later: an ordered constraint is typed ORDER only at R >= ~20. A null ORDER count is therefore an upper bound, not proof of free order. |
| pe56:PE-56.3c | Nulls W1, W2, SH1, SH2 | W1: 4 TABLET, 0 ORDER. W2 (a line-Markov world): 7 ORDER (ES M288 -> M288, M056 -> M288, M072 -> M317, M003 -> M054, ...), 7 TABLET. SH1: 5 TABLET; SH2: 2 TABLET; 0 ORDER in all shuffles | TABLET residues appear at 2-7 per null: that type is not separable from noise at this size. ORDER appears only in a world that was built with entry order. |
| pe56:PE-56.3d | Real PE (48 residues) | HEADER 33 (all 26 M157 items; |M327+M342| -> SDB, M387, M328, M319; |M327+X|): the M157 excess (R 198 vs forger 103) vanishes once the real header is copied (HDR 213, TOPIC 204). TOPIC 8 (self-repeats M354, |M036+1(N30D)|, M376, M297; LR M049 -> M371, M032 -> M005, M058 -> M372). TABLET 7: CO M003/|M195+M057| (10 vs TOPIC 0.3, PERM 5.6), CO M003/M147 (9 vs 0.3), CO M340/|M153+M342| (6 vs 0.5), EF M096 -> M390 (4), TOT M297 = running sum (5 vs PERM 1.4, VPERM 1.1, p 0.005-0.01), MM 2->2 and ?->?. ORDER 0; BINDING 0 | PE has no residue of the ORDER type (proto-cuneiform 4 and Ur III 2 at the same size; W2 7), and its TABLET count (7) lies in the null range (2-7). The M157 cycle-1 residue is a forger artefact: forgers under-produce M157 HEADERS; given the header, the content follows from any header-keyed pool. |
| pe56:PE-56.final | Novelty: la46's item-level forger inversion, rebuilt for PE lines (header, entries = sign string + numeral system + value) with 14 relation families (entry successions, ordered pairs at distance >= 2 entries, header -> entry sign, header -> number system, system pairs and successions, sign -> system / magnitude, magnitude runs, running totals, co-occurrence, last line, in-line pairs). 120 random forger architectures per corpus (n-gram back-off over abstracted streams, vocabulary-clustering forgers K 2-32, entry-level line-bank grammars with Markov succession, bag forgers; ~141,000 forged tablets per corpus, ~1.5 M in all), ensemble-mean rule. Cycle 2: residues from half A tested on unseen half B against fresh forgers. Cycle 3 (new): each residue dissected by oracle forgers given one kind of knowledge (own-entry permutation, numeral permutation, header-keyed pools, topic pools). Nulls: forger worlds W1, W2; shuffled PE SH1, SH2. Controls: planted long-range constraints PL1, PL2; Ur III and proto-cuneiform at PE token count, signs as opaque ids. | Calibration: Bonferroni passes W1 1, W2 2, SH 0 (artefacts of the worlds themselves); planted 8/10 testable found, 0 true false passes; Ur III gives back totals (TOT udu = running sum 11 vs 1.1, typed ORDER+BIND) and the date frame (LAST mu 296 vs 71, typed TABLET); proto-cuneiform gives LAST GU7 and 4 fixed entry orders that survive permutation of the tablet's own lines (MA -> HASZHUR, U4x1(N57) -> KISIM, ME -> ZATU753). PE: 9 Bonferroni passes, all 'tablets headed by M157 carry M157 more than forgers write it'; it transfers to held-out tablets at 1.85x, but the same item appears and transfers in the forger worlds (1.52-1.67x) and vanishes when the real header is copied (HDR 213 vs R 198): a forger artefact. Cycle-3 types for PE's 48 p<1e-3 residues: HEADER 33, TOPIC 8, TABLET 7 (null range 2-7), ORDER 0, BINDING 0. Held-out firing: PE 1.96 vs forger worlds 1.52-1.53, Ur III 2.81, planted constraints 5-19x. | NOT CRACKED. B (negative, upper bound): no PE relation is failed by surface forgers the way Ur III totals and dates or planted long-range constraints are; in particular no ordered entry constraint survives permutation of the tablet's own entries (0 of 48; proto-cuneiform 4, Ur III 2; method power only at R >= ~20 for a constraint at rate 0.8). Read with pe46 and pe51: PE meaning sits in which signs share a tablet and a header, not in where entries stand. B: the one whole-tablet structure forgers miss is the serial dossier (P008718-P008731: M157 / |M195+M057| M147 ... / M288 / M009 M003, copied tablet to tablet). C: M297 heads closing totals equal to the running sum (5 tablets vs 0.5 forged; would support: new M297 lines that add up; would kill: M297 lines on new tablets that do not sum). A (method): the ensemble-mean inversion needs a world-artefact check: a forger weakness that is stable (here, header production) transfers to held-out tablets at 1.5-2.7x and must be calibrated against forger worlds, not only shuffles. |
| pe61:PE-61.1a | EVERY BUREAUCRACY ON EARTH VOTES ON PROTO-ELAMITE, corpus (tools/pe61_common.py, pe61_build.py; checkpoints data/pe61_ckpt/). Port of linear-a la57: PE reduced to the same value-free form as the seven known administrations (proto-cuneiform, Ur III, Old Babylonian, Ebla, Old Assyrian, Linear B, Inca khipus): each base sign = one opaque token, entry numeral AFTER its signs (PE order), measured flag by the la57 proto-cuneiform rule (system not plain SDB, or fractional), header lines without numerals; 1,160 tablets with >= 2 numeral lines, 20,300 tokens. Features (la57's 42 position / number-adjacency / arithmetic / type-level) are computed on Linear-A-sized draws (5,219 tokens, the size every known system was trained at): 3 random partitions x 4 disjoint draws; a sign's score = mean within-draw rank over all its occurrences. Shuffles: S1 signs over all slots, S2 signs WITHIN each entry (numerals fixed), S3 line order within tablet, 12 each. pe59 frozen classes (MEASURED 14, COUNTED 12, PERSON 15, PREFIX 7, M376, M288) are used only to score. | 372 PE signs with >= 6 occurrences. | Setup. |
| pe61:PE-61.1b | Arm A (all seven administrations, proto-cuneiform included): the la57 cycle-2 SURVIVOR taggers (trained random specs that kept held-out AUC >= 0.6 on every held-out known system: COM 8, PER 72, PLA 168, TRA 72, HDR 142, UNI 176; TOT has 0 survivors and is not usable) refitted on all eligible known systems and run on PE (tools/pe61_c1.py, pe61_report.py). NULL: same specs refitted on role labels permuted inside each training corpus (5 reps). CONTROLS: la57 planted administration (lines end in a numeral, i.e. PE order); S1/S2/S3 shuffles; dumb baseline = the single feature t_adjN (share of a sign's occurrences next to a numeral). | PLANT recovered: COM AUC 0.86, TRA 0.78, PLA 0.75, PER 0.66. Agreement of the world's COMMODITY vote with pe59 MEASURED+COUNTED (sign AUC): 0.870 (MEASURED 0.898, COUNTED 0.809); label-perm null median 0.19 but max 0.92; S1 0.54; S2 0.82 (q95 0.83); S3 0.85 (q95 0.86); dumb baseline 0.78. M288 is the world's #2 commodity (pct 0.997), M376 pct 0.925. PERSON class as COM 0.51 (= baseline 0.505). UNI ranks MEAS+CNT 0.905 (S2 0.88). PER and PLA votes rank pe59 PERSON at 0.33 / 0.36 (BELOW chance), MEAS+CNT at 0.10 (anti-commodity); their spread and majority share equal the S3 shuffles (sd 0.149 vs 0.148; majority 3.2% vs 3.7%). TRA and HDR rank PREFIX/PERSON high (0.82-0.93) but S1 global shuffles give the same (0.74-0.89): frequency, not role. Top COM signs: M418, M288, M354 (ag 1.00), M482, M268, |M036+1(N30D)|, M036, M002, M050, M010, M367, M283, M088, |M039+1(N30D)|. | The world's commodity vote and pe59 MEASURED/COUNTED agree (0.87), but nearly all of it is 'stands right before a numeral' (baseline 0.78; S2/S3 keep 0.82-0.85; one label-permuted model set reaches 0.92). PE persons, places and transactions get no vote above shuffles (B, negative, as on Linear A). pe59 PERSON signs are NOT called persons by any outside bureaucracy (C-: they are units being counted, consistent with pe59's 'person-like unit'). |
| pe61:PE-61.1c | Beyond-layout check (tools/pe61_resid.py): sign-level role scores regressed on 7 layout covariates (t_adjN, frequency, line-final, document-first, position, adjN, lfin); residual AUC vs pe59 classes; and pe59 MEAS+CNT vs PERSON among number-adjacent signs only (26 vs 15 class signs; 35 adjacent). Same for label-permuted nulls and S2/S3. Plus single-feature baselines for the adjacent contrast. | COM residual MC 0.682 (null 0.34-0.65; S2 q95 0.681; S3 q95 0.664): just above all. COM MEAS+CNT vs PERSON among number-adjacent signs 0.910 (null 0.14-0.79; S2 0.87 q95 0.93; S3 0.87 q95 0.89). Single raw features already give 0.893 (nrank = rank of the adjacent number in the tablet), 0.876 (nextN), 0.868 (mag). UNI 0.769 (S3 q95 0.757). PER/PLA/TRA/HDR adjacent contrasts are below 0.3 (they call persons what COM does not: mirror image). | The world's commodity vote separates pe59 commodity classes from pe59 PERSON units because commodity lines carry the BIGGER numbers (nrank alone 0.893 vs ensemble 0.910). B: in PE, as in every known administration, the commodity sign is the one carrying the largest quantity directly before it; the outside vote adds ~0.02 AUC beyond that one feature (C, not significant against S2). |
| pe61:PE-61.2a | Arm B, PROTO-CUNEIFORM NEVER SEEN (tools/pe61_c2.py): 200 fresh random trained taggers per role (2-15 of 42 features x LR / tree / NB), selected by leave-one-system-out over the known systems EXCEPT proto-cuneiform (survivor = held-out AUC >= 0.6 on every non-PC system); null = labels permuted in every training corpus (3 reps, same specs). Survivors refitted on all non-PC systems and scored on (i) proto-cuneiform with its conventional role labels (the hardest held-out control: PE's neighbour), (ii) proto-cuneiform re-laid out in PE order (numeral after the signs), (iii) the planted administration, (iv) PE and its S1/S2/S3 shuffles. Roles: COM (UR3 OB EB OA LB) and TRA (UR3 OB EB LB). | Survivors: COM 24 (null 0, 0, 0); TRA 117 (null 94, 19, 63: no signal). PLANT: COM 0.958, TRA 0.867. Proto-cuneiform held out: COM per-model AUC median 0.572, 38% of models >= 0.6 (label-perm null medians 0.47 / 0.44 / 0.50); in PE order 0.571 (null 0.45-0.50). TRA on PC 0.584 vs null 0.52-0.56. | COM transfers to the never-seen neighbour, but weakly (0.57 against null 0.44-0.50; negatives there are hard: units, offices, transaction signs). Moving the numeral after the signs changes nothing (0.571), so PE's entry order is not a barrier. TRA does not survive its null (as in la57). |
| pe61:PE-61.2b | Arm B read-out on PE vs pe59 frozen classes (tools/pe61_report.py c2, pe61_resid.py c2), and arm A vs arm B as the leakage gauge. | World commodity vote (no PC) vs pe59 MEASURED+COUNTED: 0.901 (MEASURED 0.876, COUNTED 0.897) vs label-perm null max 0.473, S1 q95 0.707, S2 q95 0.886, S3 q95 0.879, dumb baseline 0.782: above every null and shuffle. Residual after 7 layout covariates 0.660 (S2 q95 0.674, S3 q95 0.618; null 0.41-0.51). But pe59 PERSON signs now score 0.725 as COM (arm A 0.513), and MEAS+CNT vs PERSON among number-adjacent signs drops from 0.910 (arm A) to 0.688 (S2 q95 0.718, S3 q95 0.665; null 0.19-0.23). Arm A vs arm B COM sign scores: Spearman 0.82 (372 signs); 23 of the top 37 shared. M288 #1, M376 pct 0.987. Top COM (no PC): M288, |M288+1(N01)|, M346, M367, M354, M376, M418, |M217+M388|, M297, M036, M264, |M036+1(N30D)|, M072, M268. | B: the world's commodity role, learned without proto-cuneiform, finds pe59's MEASURED and COUNTED classes in PE above label-permuted, within-entry and line-order shuffles (0.90 vs <= 0.89). It is largely a 'big number right after me' detector, so it is independent support for pe59's class-sign layer, not for any meaning. B (negative / leakage): the commodity-vs-PERSON split (0.91) needs proto-cuneiform in training; without it the world calls pe59's PERSON signs commodities too (0.725). Outside bureaucracies count persons as goods; only PE's neighbour separates them. C: shared top-decile commodity signs missing from pe59's lists: M354, M367, M418, M482, M268, M269, M088, M069, M206, |M036+1(N30D)|, |M288+1(N01)| (would support: they take capacity or count numerals at class-sign rates on new tablets; would kill: they head entries or sit in name strings at >= the PERSON rate). |
| pe61:PE-61.3 | A PARLIAMENT OF BUREAUCRACIES VOTES ON PROTO-ELAMITE (tools/pe61_c3.py, pe61_report3.py). Each administration trains its OWN 40 random experts on its own labels only (la57_c3 style) and votes on every PE sign; Linear A sits as a COM voter in its structural form (logogram tokens vs syllabic words, admin documents) and the khipus vote on TOTAL (top cords). NULL: voters trained on labels permuted inside their own corpus (2 reps, same specs); PE S1/S2/S3 shuffles (6 each); planted administration per voter; proto-cuneiform re-laid out in PE order for non-PC voters. Verdict = a majority of voters put the sign in their top decile; PC's vote shown separately as the leakage gauge. 111 voter fits x 40 experts. | COM (7 voters): AUC vs pe59 MEAS+CNT: UR3 0.88, OB 0.89, EB 0.90, OA 0.74, LB 0.92, LA 0.91 (S3 0.66-0.89), but PC 0.42, and PC's own voter fails the planted control (0.50) so its dissent is uninformative. Majority-voted signs 26 of 372 (null voters 1, 10; S1 19, S2 max 18, S3 max 19); inter-voter rho 0.465 = shuffles (S3 0.459). Majority COM: M269 6/7, M054 6/7, |M288+1(N01)|, M418, M288, M354, M264, M367, M036, M346, M376, M072, M069, |M036+1(N30D)|, M206, M010 (5/7). UNI (cuneiform only, 5 voters): 32 majority signs (shuffle max 25; null 10, 1), the same signs as COM (M482, M418, M288, M367, M036, M354, M297, M346, M269, M362, M002, M376, M072 all 5/5). TOT (UR3 OB OA LB KH): 18 majority signs = null voters 18, 18; rho 0.05; KH 0.43 on M288. PER: 22 (S3 max 20), PLA: 16 (S1 median 19), TRA: 19 (null 21): none above controls; PER voters rank pe59 PERSON at 0.17-0.37 except EB 0.80. HDR: 25 (S3 max 22; rho 0.51 < S3 0.59): M388, M387, M057, M124, M066, M004, M024 4/5. | B: every written bureaucracy that has its own commodity experts (Ur III, OB, Ebla, Old Assyrian, Linear B, Linear A's logogram form) calls pe59's MEASURED/COUNTED class signs commodities; the parliament's commodity majority (26 signs) exceeds every shuffle (<= 19). In PE, 'commodity' and 'unit' collapse onto one class-sign layer (as Linear A's 'word welded to a quantity'). A (calibrated negative): no total sign (TOT majority = permuted-label voters, 18 = 18), matching pe59's frozen 'totals have no sign' (A); persons, places and transactions get no vote above shuffles. C: header-role voters pick pe59 PERSON/PREFIX signs M388, M387, M124, M057, M066 plus M004, M024, M240, M352 as 'office/header'-like (only just above S3 on count, below it on agreement). C: M054 (pe59 PERSON) and M269 are commodities to 6 of 7 voters (would support: M054 taking capacity numerals or fractions on new tablets; would kill: M054 lines followed by M288 allotments at the PERSON rate). |
| pe63:PE-63.1a | Template search. A random template = 1-4 features drawn (rarity-weighted) from one random tablet: an exact line (sign set + number system), a sign, the header sign, the closing line, the line-type skeleton (header/system sequence), the line count, or a block of 6 consecutive CDLI numbers (two offsets). The group = every tablet with all the features (3-40 tablets). Score = HELD-OUT cohesion: mean pairwise tablet-calibrated z of IDF-weighted content similarity (line keys + signs) after removing everything the template itself defines. 1,000,000 templates per run. NULL: the same search on 3 line-shuffled corpora (every line moved to a random tablet slot of the same kind and position class; lengths and line frequencies kept). A group counts only if it beats the maximum of all 3 nulls (3 M templates) in its size class (3 / 4-5 / 6-9 / 10+). Dossiers = greedy merge of significant groups (a later group joins when >= 2 and >= 1/3 of its members are in one dossier; a new member must have mean pairwise z >= 3 to it; no chaining) | PE: 6,862 distinct groups; family-wise thresholds 9.1 / 6.2 / 5.6 / 4.0; 50 significant groups vs 1-5 in each null run scored against the other two nulls (1, 5, 1). | The real corpus has 10-50x more near-copy groups than line-shuffled corpora. |
| pe63:PE-63.1b | CONTROL planted dossiers: 5 seeds; a real tablet (5-12 lines) copied 6 times with one sign swapped and number fixed (slot A), one sign swapped with a new number system and scale (slot B), one sign swapped with a x2 quantity step (slot C); planted copies get no museum adjacency. 300,000 templates each, PE null thresholds | recovered 30 of 30 planted tablets (6/6 in every seed); extra members 2-5 per seed, always including the source tablet itself (a true near-copy) | PASS (grade A, method finds a planted dossier at PE size). |
| pe63:PE-63.1c | CONTROL Ur III: 1,500 random Drehem tablets and 1,500 random Umma tablets (CDLI, words as opaque tokens, numerals outside the tokens), same search, 2 line-shuffled nulls each. Validation not used by the search: share of member pairs with the same month name and the same year name | Drehem: 129 significant groups, 21 dossiers (152 tablets); same year 44% vs 2.3% random pairs, same month 9.6% vs 5.9%. Umma: 629 groups, 62 dossiers (398 tablets); same year 14% vs 1.1%, same month 6.0% vs 1.7%. Null runs scored against the other null: 2-5 groups | PASS: dossiers in a known administration are real date-bound series (partly circular: year words can enter a template). Ur III is 3-12x richer in dossiers than PE per tablet (PE 42/1,201 = 3.5%; Drehem 10%; Umma 27%). |

| pe63:PE-63.final | Idea: treat tablets copied from one template as a laboratory. Cycle 1: 1 M random templates per corpus (shared lines, signs, header, closing line, skeleton, line count, consecutive CDLI numbers), scored on held-out cohesion against 3 line-shuffled corpora (family-wise max per size class). Cycle 2: align the copies; type each slot by what changes with it (swap alone = identifier, swap with unit/scale = commodity, regular step = time/rank, coupled quantity = rate). Cycle 3: test the roles outside the dossiers and against pe59. Controls: planted dossiers (30/30 tablets, roles 15/15, planted rate 4/4, no false couplings); Ur III Drehem and Umma with opaque words (dossiers share year names 44% / 14% vs 1-2%; months and persons read as identifiers, commodities as quantity slots, p < 0.0002; roles transfer to the rest of the archive, p < 0.0002) | PE: 8 dossiers, 42 tablets (D1 P008717-P008731 serial run; D2 P008796-P008802; D3 P009190 ... P009309; D4 P009056/P009137-P009140; D5 P008790-P008794; D6 P008100, P008125, P008193, P368479; D7 Tepe Sofalin TSF 001-003). Headers fixed or swapped alone; name-like strings change on every copy; counts vary; no time/rank steps, no totals; two per-unit rates (D3 M288 = n/2 N01 = 60 N39C per unit 6/6, D2 48 N39C = 2 N39B per unit 5/6). Outside the dossiers the ID/quantity split does not transfer (p 0.29 / 0.11); M001/M286 = M288 is killed (3/47 vs 3.7); M009 M003 is used only by the serial run (14 tablets, 0 elsewhere) | NOT CRACKED. B: PE has a few genuine template series, listed above (3.5% of tablets; Ur III 3-8x more). B: inside series, PE varies identifiers and counts, never units or steps; the M288 line follows the count at fixed rates (60 per unit repeats pe27/28, B; 48 per unit holds across D2, C+). B- (negative, calibrated): dossier minimal pairs do not type PE signs outside the dossiers. C: M009 M003 is a series-specific closing tag (would kill: M009 M003 on tablets of another template). Killed: M001/M286 interchangeable with M288. |
